Account collection can use multiple external calls. Endpoint names alone don't establish the completed ISC responsibility. These are connector-specific supporting examples, not a complete InvoiceDesk configuration or a claim every connector uses the same calls. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match their inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 48 and 37 characters. The first separates account-held IDs from object definitions and shows their logical information relationship. It doesn't claim a private storage implementation, simultaneous execution, universal run order or external grant. The second is a separate UTC observation/change timeline, not internal aggregation stages.

Devi 52002 reviews invoices. Existing account inv-52002 has employeeRef 52002 and a supplied verified identity association. It holds G-520 and G-521. inv-52003 holds G-520; inv-52004 holds G-521. In the stated complete selected population, 3 accounts produce 4 membership occurrences and 2 distinct held values. The group read returns 3 definitions: G-520 Invoice Reviewers, G-521 Archive Readers and unused G-599 Exception Reviewers.

The selected JSON account object and three-element group array are valid teaching data, not complete ISC API responses/configuration. The account's groups attribute is entitlement-marked and connected to groups; groupKey supplies Entitlement ID, label supplies Entitlement Name. Two account-created representations gain descriptions and the group read adds knowledge of G-599, under the supplied no-manual-description-block assumption. These aren't 3 new grants.

The timeline is independent of the main completed trace: account observation 09:00 UTC, target removal of Devi's G-520 membership 09:20, group existence observation 09:30. The later object read doesn't establish refreshed account membership. Counterfactual mapping, denied group read, manual description and selected-type cases aren't simultaneous main defects. T-22/T-90 is an independent project-file transfer.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Three accounts, four memberships | Account, relationship, distinct-value and object totals must agree | Trace the stated rows: 3 accounts, 4 occurrences, 2 held group IDs, 3 definitions. These count different things. |
| New inventory object | Discovering a group assigns it to the account | G-599 is an object without a supplied Devi membership; no grant operation is given. |
| Membership before description | Missing rich details invalidate an account-held value | Establish supported object collection, input, scope and mappings; don't substitute repeated account jobs for descriptive-data investigation. |
| Label versus ID | Another object read repairs an account-side mapping mismatch | Compare Invoice Reviewers with designated G-520; preserve intended identifiers and review existing references before schema redesign. |
| Account read succeeds, group read denied | Read permission or success applies to every operation | Investigate the supplied group-read authorization with the authorized owner; don't change memberships to repair inventory. |
| Later group read | Most recent job refreshes all related facts | Preserve independent observations; group existence at 09:30 doesn't refresh 09:00 account membership or reverse the 09:20 target removal. |
| Manual description retained | Old visible wording proves a failed account read | Check delivered object, mapping, manual-update state and responsibility; preservation can be intended, not a read failure. |
| One type finishes | Groups-only success establishes all supported access types | Verify separately included type, schema, actor visibility, scope and results; no unselected application-role completion is inferred. |
| Project-file transfer | Object inventory assigns every row and supplies a native recurring flat-file schedule | Preserve T-22 membership and distinguish inventory-only T-90. Establish account owner, template, scopes/times and actual imports. Reject unsupported schedule; a new membership requires a separate authorized change mechanism and target evidence. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.


+## AGG-003: Full aggregation, optimization and reprocessing unchanged records

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 5.3.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Official account-loading modes, API initiation/request lifetime, history/Search and single-account boundaries; account correlation including manual associations; manager correlation; V2025 account-aggregation request; identity processing/profile mappings; and source/schema/provisioning responsibilities freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for this scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant/API execution: not performed. Employee 53003, AssetDesk Production, all account/identity values, comparison correction, read counts and association outcomes are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: distinguish full configured-population reading from unchanged-record handling, then trace a changed comparison against unchanged input. Detailed delta mechanisms, single-account behavior, schedules, deletion handling and correlation/identity-processing design retain their primary lessons. No real account, source setting, identity, credential or access is changed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Optimized default reads records and skips further processing/correlation for existing unchanged data; unoptimized reads re-evaluate unchanged records. Documented disableOptimization=true is run-local and UI scheduled/manual runs remain optimized. History exposes execution/optimization context; Search freshness, single-account limits and deletion safeguards aren't replaced by a successful rerun. No checksum, runtime or private execution model is invented. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured account-to-identity attribute comparisons and documented disabled-optimization re-examination after configuration changes. Manual associations aren't automatically moved because attributes change. The successful case is initially uncorrelated with one supplied unique existing match and no manual override, not a universal reassignment mechanism. |
| [Configuring manager correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/manager_correlation.html) | Links employee identity to manager identity, distinct from account ownership. No complete manager configuration, profile-priority algorithm or manager outcome is invented for AssetDesk. |
| [V2025 account aggregation API](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/api/v2025/import-accounts/) | Current primary request specification: source-scoped aggregation, multipart/form-data disableOptimization string field, supported authentication/authorization and 202 task response. The documentation entry redirects to its generated reference; the selected operation was inspected there. The admin guide's beta-route example isn't copied into a runnable lesson. No API call, source patch, token generation or JSON account edit is performed. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Event-based processing when aggregation/provisioning modifies an identity; manual processing applies identity-profile/access-model changes even without identity data changes. Starting processing doesn't validate target provisioning success. Profile-change transfer asks for the appropriate scoped mechanism, not indiscriminate re-reading of all applications. |
| [Creating identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Profile-backed population and attribute mapping responsibilities. Office mapping in the transfer uses existing collected inputs; no new source data, authoritative feed or target-write result is assumed. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source/connector distinctions, source Accounts views and explicit uncorrelated shadow representation. Placeholder isn't proof of another HR-established employee. Correct source/account context and actual association are separate from a read-status label. |
| [Managing account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Represented attribute structure and identification responsibilities. Stable valid account identification is supplied. A target-only comment outside the returned/represented data doesn't establish a changed collected record; no private comparison algorithm is inferred. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | External account/access changes require their supported change mechanism. Correct ownership isn't a successful target grant. Read/processing can feed configured downstream changes, so the whole workflow isn't characterized as universally harmless. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match the inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 43 and 41 characters. The decision diagram covers new/changed records versus existing unchanged records and the optimization choice. It is a logical processing model, not a checksum, queue, host, transaction sequence or guarantee of successful ownership. The second is a comparison of two sequential teaching executions, not a task screenshot or universal counter-name mapping.

Devi 53003 coordinates equipment requests. Existing AssetDesk account asset-53003 has mail devi.old@example.invalid, employeeRef text 53003 and department OPS. Existing identity work email is devi.current@example.invalid; employeeNumber text is 53003. The email mismatch already exists at initial collection, so no automatic removal of a previously correct association is implied. Old criterion compares mail with work email; corrected criterion compares employeeRef with employeeNumber. Input account values remain unchanged.

Run A's configured population contains four fully delivered records: existing unchanged asset-53003 and asset-53005, changed asset-53004 department OPS-to-FIN, and externally existing asset-53006 first represented in ISC. Optimized handling reads 4, processes 2 and skips 2. Following Run A, no further external/mapped input changes occur; Run B disables optimization, reads 4 and reprocesses 4 without unchanged skips. These are supplied logical record counts. Corrected asset-53003 ownership is an explicit verified fictional outcome under unique-match/no-manual-override assumptions.

The selected JSON is valid and isn't a full ISC API object or runnable request. example.invalid addresses aren't real contacts. Counterfactual 53030 mismatch, absent delta delivery, manual ownership and denied target-write cases are independent of the successful correction. HR/profile transfer uses a different configuration-change problem with current account inputs already present.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Four read, two skipped | Scanned population equals repeated processing | Trace the four supplied states: changed 53004 and new representation 53006 process; unchanged 53003/53005 skip; 4 = 2 + 2. |
| Saved comparison, unchanged account | Correcting configuration makes an account value change or requires a fake edit | Compare approved criteria and actual matching values, then establish supported unchanged-record re-evaluation; don't alter correct source data to force a change. |
| Target-only comment change | Any application-screen edit establishes changed data in ISC's processing decision | Compare delivered/represented fields and schema/mappings; supplied input hasn't changed. No hidden checksum behavior is asserted. |
| Previous run's option | disableOptimization permanently changes the source or future UI schedules | Inspect each execution; option applies only to its requested aggregation, while documented UI behavior stays optimized. |
| Delta omits the account | Reprocessing options establish delivery under any read scope | Establish a supported read including the record and the relevant processing choice separately; no universal delta-token reset is inferred. |
| Mismatched value reprocessed | Re-evaluation repairs wrong inputs or guarantees a match | 53030 differs from 53003; investigate approved values/configuration and actual association, not silent identifier replacement. |
| Manual ownership | Unoptimized reads automatically move every previously associated account | Main case has no manual override; verify the distinct association and documented authorized handling rather than promising mass reassignment or resetting a source. |
| Correct association, denied write | Successful account processing proves target delivery | Ownership succeeded; separate provisioning failed. Inspect the actual operation/authority/target state without substituting aggregation success. |
| Identity-profile transfer | Forced source aggregation is the universal response to every ISC configuration change | With current source inputs, establish affected profile/mapping and supported manual identity processing. Collect fresh data separately only if needed; verify identity output and any separate target consequences. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

- Scope: changes-only collection and the dependencies behind coverage. Detailed deletion safeguards, schedules, single-account operations and source reprocessing retain their primary lessons. No SQL, API, credential, permission, tracking-state or real account change is executed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Supported delta versus full/optimized/unoptimized responsibilities, source-level selection and execution/history/Search evidence. Explicit OU-move limitation calls for full aggregation. A delta result isn't a complete current inventory; a processing option doesn't establish delivery of an account omitted from a changes-only read. No universal timing, scope reset or retry behavior is invented. |
| [JDBC SaaS delta aggregation tables](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/jdbc/help/saas_connectivity/jdbc/delta_agg.html) | Initial full aggregation, account table plus delta table, identifying value/action columns, Insert/Update/Delete actions, triggers and tracking read/write/cleanup requirements; tracking re-initialization described after aggregation. Main case follows this SaaS table model, not an IdentityIQ recipe; its exact table/column names and completed deletion result are fictional supplied conditions. No private cleanup commit timing is claimed. |
| [Active Directory delta aggregation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/delta_aggregation.html) | Default DirSync change mechanism, supported added/updated/deleted changes and initial full-reference establishment. | [Active Directory delta permissions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/delta_aggregation_permissions.html) | Additional Replicating Directory Changes and Deleted Objects container read access. Ordinary account visibility/connection success doesn't establish these permissions. No permission commands or security changes are performed or reproduced as a project recipe. |
| [Okta SaaS delta aggregation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/okta/help/saas_connectivity/okta/delta_aggregation.html) | Accounts-only delta; users API handles user-profile changes, logs API supplies entitlement/deleted-user changes, with documented 90-day log-retention boundary and required full-read recovery for unavailable older changes. Current-state recovery isn't reconstruction of expired historical events. No IdentityIQ parameters or universal cookie lifetime are imported. |
| [Managing source account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Represented account attributes and identification. Native wq-54004 differs from employeeRef 54004; stable valid account identification is supplied. A change row and final account representation aren't interchangeable data shapes; no generic omission-means-clear behavior is invented. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source/connector responsibilities and source Accounts views/associations. The lesson connects those records to delivered data and supplied outcomes, not an employee-name search or a schema definition alone. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Ownership association uses configured attribute comparisons; changed criteria need appropriate re-evaluation. Devi's existing correct association is explicit. Equal digits, successful collection or delta configuration don't establish a new unique owner. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Aggregation/provisioning changes to an identity initiate processing; mapped identity values and configured access consequences remain distinct from source account data. No every-delta-row/every-identity-calculation or completed-target-write guarantee is made. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | External account/access changes use their supported change mechanism. Delta observes already-performed WorkQueue changes; it doesn't repeat external create/delete actions. Tracking-table bookkeeping isn't unrestricted business-data provisioning, and downstream consequences require separate verification. |

### Diagram, data and reasoning checks

Two editable text assets match their inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization. Maximum line widths are 38 and 40 characters. The first shows database capture, connector read and ISC handling as logical collection dependencies. The separated engineer line is human verification, not an automatic success stage or private transaction guarantee. The second displays supplied before/observed/after account representations, not commands sent to WorkQueue.

Devi 54004 coordinates service work. Existing account wq-54004 has employeeRef text 54004 and a supplied verified identity association. Its department changes OPS to FIN. WorkQueue Production uses a fictional supported JDBC SaaS account-table/delta-table design: WORKQUEUE_ACCOUNTS and WORKQUEUE_DELTA. accountKey is the external account identifier; action values are Update, Insert and Delete, not universal column defaults.

Completed baseline contains wq-54004, wq-54005 and wq-54006. Continuous correct capture records Update wq-54004, Insert externally created wq-54007 and Delete externally removed wq-54006. Supplied supported handling represents FIN on Devi's account, preserves omitted unchanged wq-54005, represents wq-54007 and removes the ISC representation of wq-54006. Current inventory contains wq-54004, wq-54005 and wq-54007: 3 + 1 - 1 = 3. The 3 change rows aren't 3 live account objects or a full population.

Selected JSON is valid teaching data, not a full ISC API object. No change-table deletion/reset command is supplied. Failed trigger, partial-page/progress, filter expansion, joined-membership-table, expired Okta logs and corrected ISC comparison are counterfactual cases, not simultaneous defects in the completed main trace. STAFF/STAFF_GROUPS is an independent project transfer.

A baseline/capture transition gap, missing change entry, unknown checkpoint state or partial response is treated as insufficient evidence rather than a universal product failure algorithm. No exactly-once replay, global rollback, atomic update/cleanup, universal timestamp boundary or token lifetime is asserted.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Unchanged account omitted | Absence from delta means account deletion | wq-54005 remains in the established representation; explicit supported deletion information and context differ from omission. |
| Empty result after failed capture | Successful collection proves no application changes | Inspect the supplied trigger/capture gap and actual row/query; repeated incomplete change reads don't reconstruct missing evidence. |
| Three change rows | Delta count equals live population or new accounts | One Update, one Insert and one Delete change the inventory contents; retain 54004/54005, add 54007, remove 54006 representation, with 3 remaining. |
| Interrupted paged read | Advancing a timestamp is a safe universal recovery or all work rolls back | Inspect execution, change evidence and actual outcomes; follow connector-specific recovery without inventing atomic completion or exactly-once replay. |
| Expanded account filter | A broader configuration automatically delivers unchanged newly included accounts | Establish supported population refresh/reinitialization and scope consequences; changed filter isn't delivery evidence. |
| Membership table changes | Correct joined account query implies complete change capture | Capture must select affected account keys for the supported relationship; group inventory doesn't substitute for a missing account-membership observation. |
| Logs outside retention | Repeated delta reads recover expired events | Follow documented Okta SaaS full-read recovery for current state with scope review; don't claim restored historical events. |
| ISC-only comparison change | Disabling optimization makes an undelivered delta record arrive | Establish both supported record coverage and appropriate re-evaluation; input/configuration and read/processing boundaries remain separate. |
| Database-design transfer | Connection success establishes all capture and missing means deleted; tracking can be cleared after failure | Inspect account keys, baseline, query, deletion/membership capture and least necessary supported permissions. Reject absence-based deletion and loss of unread evidence; verify documented recovery/current results separately from lost history and target writes. |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Source evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), Aggregating Data for a Single Account | Primary UI contract: admin/helpdesk can refresh one account's data/status; documented Identities > Accounts > Aggregate Account context. This is not generalized to every API or used to deny connector-specific explicit delta deletion support. |

| [Web Services SaaS HTTP operations](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/http_operations.html) | Distinct Test Connection, Account Aggregation, Get Object, Group Aggregation and account-change operations. | [Account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Configured account fields and selected account identification. accountKey, employeeRef, locked and office are illustrative project fields, not complete API objects or universal mapping conventions. |

In this example office explicitly uses the CaseHub account input, making the UI no-sync boundary meaningful; no intentional HR-office override explains the unchanged main value. The existing identity/association is supplied. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html), manual processing | Profile/access configuration changes can require manual identity processing even when source data is unchanged; selected-identity processing is separate. Its scope and possible consequences must be reviewed. Generic processing descriptions are not used to override the explicit single-account UI limits. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Ownership matching is a distinct responsibility with required input/comparison context. A successful specified-account refresh is not a documented promise of universal ownership or manual-association repair. |
| [Official Accounts TypeScript SDK reference](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/tools/sdk/typescript/accounts/methods/accounts/) | Fresh current operations distinguish stored account-details retrieval, asynchronous connector reload/one-time aggregation returning AccountsAsyncResult, and separate unlock. Account id parameter is not the external native key or employee number. Exact routes, scope lists and API versions deliberately omitted from learner prose because they are not needed for this theory distinction and older v3/beta pages differ. No API executed or API/UI side-effect equivalence asserted. |

### Example and diagram consistency

Devi 55005 coordinates support cases. Main case has one existing correctly associated CaseHub Production account ch-55005. A supported Web Services SaaS lookup returns selected JSON accountKey ch-55005, employeeRef text 55005, locked Boolean true and office HYD. Before the UI action, external data is locked/HYD and ISC account data is unlocked/BLR; identity office is BLR with an existing profile mapping from this account. After the supplied successful action, ISC account data is locked/HYD, identity office remains BLR and external lock remains true. Neither an unlock nor identity-attribute synchronization is inferred.

The ISC account object ID is explained but its actual generated value is not invented. Account key and employee number identify different objects. ch-55005-admin appears only in a separate two-account variation. Later unlock, not-found, acceptance, lookup-denial, file-age and permission cases are independent supplied outcomes, not simultaneous defects in the main trace. The transfer manager input is explicitly HR, not CaseHub.

Two editable assets exactly match the inline text blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization: selected-account-to-representation (maximum 31 characters) and account/identity/target boundaries (34). The first shows logical read dependencies with a separated human verification line; the second connects the UI action only to account representation and separately states unchanged identity/target outcomes. No queue, transaction, retry, network call count, universal order or automatic follow-on process is asserted. Visual rendering must also be checked in the published format.

Selected JSON parses and contains four stated fields. Two learner tables keep identity, account, external state and identifier roles separate. No real credentials, URLs, tenant output, access grant or deletion instruction is embedded in learner prose.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning and evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Locked after refresh | Read failure inferred from continued sign-in failure | Account evidence updated correctly; unlock is a separately authorized external change. Inspect operation and target evidence, not sign-in alone. |
| Account office HYD, identity BLR | Correct mapping means every operation must sync the identity | This UI action has no identity-attribute synchronization. Check actual input/mapping and choose the supported identity-update process; do not edit a correct mapping as a workaround. |
| Not found, representation retained | Existing ISC record proves external existence or implies deletion | Retention is this action's documented behavior. Establish source/key/lookup/actor facts and use supported deletion handling with safeguards if needed. |
| Administrative account selected | One person's account action refreshes every related account | Confirm the selected source and external key; ordinary account was not selected. |
| Async acceptance then old stored data | Acceptance proves completed collection, or stored GET forces a source read | Obtain completion/outcome evidence. A task reference and stored-account retrieval are different from completed connector reload. |
| Listing succeeds, lookup denied | List authority proves lookup authority, or field mapping causes an explicit permission denial | Supplied denied lookup lacks required permission. Resolve approved operation-specific authority before diagnosing field extraction. Least-privilege alternatives are acceptable. |
| New import of old export | Load time establishes source freshness | Export time/content bound the imported data. Fresh supported input or a separately designed live integration is needed. |
| Unlocked but missing case-closing permission | Unlocked means authorized, and reading grants missing access | Successful sign-in and denied function distinguish authentication from authorization. Use approved access/provisioning and verify delivery and application state. |
| Combined support button transfer | One refresh safely performs identity sync, HR manager update, unlock and deletion | Separate operation contracts, source-of-value relationships, authorities, completion checks and deletion policy. Equivalent supported designs are acceptable only with these distinct effects/evidence established. |

Questions, supplied facts and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. No beginner read-through or transfer test has been performed; author/source review does not establish learner comprehension.

## AGG-006: Schedules, scope, dependencies and data freshness

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 5.6.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Account-loading scheduling/modes/history/Search, feature-specific time zones, entitlement schedules/files, identity processing, profile mappings, account schemas, connector runtime distinctions and migration time-basis guidance freshly checked.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for this scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. PeopleRoster, worker data, published batches, all schedules/times, clock offsets and supplied identity/target outcomes are teaching examples.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: scheduling and evidence of freshness. No source schedule, tenant time zone, VA setting, credential, API, source or target record is changed. Detailed account-deletion settings retain their primary lesson.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), scheduling, methods and history sections | Scheduled time adds work to processing queue; other work can delay actual start. Direct-connect enable/frequency/time settings; daily recurrences end at midnight, weekly day/time, monthly date 1-28; documented 14:00/eight-hour pattern. Full/optimized/unoptimized/delta distinctions and source/history counts/status/start/stop/optimization evidence. Flat-file account loading requires upload or latest uploaded file, not a live producer read. No universal run-overlap, merge, skip or retry rule is inferred. |
| [Time Zone Settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/time-zones.html) | Feature-specific time basis: VA-based account/entitlement aggregation uses cluster fixed standard-time GMT offset without daylight-saving shifts; SaaS uses tenant time zone; relevant browser/activity displays can use local settings. Tenant-zone tasks can follow their zone's daylight-saving changes. This specific runtime distinction qualifies the broader VA notes in account/entitlement scheduling pages; no all-connectors-share-one-clock claim. |
| [Aggregating entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/aggregating_entitlements.html) | Supported direct-connect manual or recurring entitlement collection, all/selected types, separate controls and queued execution. Flat-file entitlement imports are manual and do not provide recurring schedule or multiple entitlement types. Independent account/object collection establishes different facts and observation times. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Event-based processing after identity changes from aggregation/provisioning, separate scheduled/time-based/manual mechanisms and configuration evaluation. Identity processing does not validate successful external provisioning. The lesson deliberately avoids a universal fixed completion duration or a full schedule/nextProcessing configuration recipe. No claim that every source read initiates every identity or access action. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html), mapping values | Source and source-attribute mapping drives identity values. Existing correct PeopleRoster association and department mapping are supplied. A late producer publication is not evidence of a faulty mapping; identity confirmation is separate evidence from source execution status. |
| [Account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Represented fields and account identification. pr-56006 is the fictional workerKey, employeeNumber text 56006 identifies employee information. HR account representation need not mean a login; it remains separate from the identity and downstream application accounts. |
| [Connector documentation overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/isc/landingpages/help/landingpages/isc_landing.html) | Runtime families and supported collection/change responsibilities. VA-based versus SaaS-based connection path is material to time-zone selection. PeopleRoster's SaaS interface and publication contract are fictional supported-design assumptions, not a claim that this named connector exists. |
| [Migrating VA-based sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/migrate_sources.html), time-zone warning before schema handling | VA cluster time basis can differ from SaaS and affect aggregation due times. The lesson asks for a review before migration; no migration is performed or irreversible migration instruction supplied. |

### Example, arithmetic and diagram review

Devi 56006 plans warehouse work. HR changes recorded department from Warehouse Planning WH to Distribution Planning DIST at 03:00. PeopleRoster Production reads the producer's published worker interface through a supplied supported SaaS design, not by uploading a CSV or reading the HR screen. Main record pr-56006 has workerKey pr-56006 and employeeNumber text 56006, a correct existing identity association and identity-profile department mapping. Tenant UTC and daily 00:10/hourly schedule are explicit.

First due/start/read/end: 03:10/03:15/03:17/03:22, with WH returned and WH represented. HR publishes DIST batch at 03:40. Second due/start/read/end: 04:10/04:15/04:18/04:24; returned account DIST is confirmed at 04:24 and mapped identity DIST separately confirmed at 04:26. Confirmation time is not asserted to be exact persistence/processing completion time. The illustration does not require all identities to wait for the source task to finish.

Change-to-confirmation = 86 minutes = 40 before publication + 30 to next scheduled submission + 5 waiting to start + 9 execution + 2 to confirmation. At 04:30, business event/publication/read/confirmation ages are 90/50/12/4 minutes. Publication age does not prove snapshot cutoff or per-field modification age. Intervals explain this trace, not a universal serial latency formula or maximum.

Independent daily 14:00 plus eight-hour recurrence gives 14:00/22:00 then next-day 14:00, a 16-hour scheduled gap. After a record has been passed at 22:00 and is changed at 22:01, wait to next due time is 15 hours 59 minutes. GMT-06 cluster 02:00 = 08:00 UTC = 03:00 at business GMT-05; the example is a fixed-offset arithmetic illustration, not a changed tenant setting.

Independent population 100 versus collected 90, G-56 membership at 09:20 between account 09:00 and group 09:30 reads, application read 05:05 before confirmed provisioning 05:12, stale Search and old export are variations, not simultaneous problems in the PeopleRoster trace. Contractor transfer uses its own hourly publication/collection design and 30-minute target-disable requirement.

Two editable text assets match inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization: publication/two-read trace has maximum line width 30; conditional dependency chain has 37. First arrows show chronology of the example, not private commit order or atomic snapshots. Value confirmations are human evidence checks. Second arrows indicate required input/outcome and explicit conditions before target observation; no automatic every-read provisioning promise or universal source serialization. Visual rendering must be separately checked in the published format.

Two learner tables retain all events and identify which age is being measured. No invented universal queue algorithm, retry limit, concurrency policy, source performance figure or live tenant evidence is supplied.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning / evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Successful run before publication | Old represented department proves mapping failure | Correct collection cannot retrieve an unpublished batch. Inspect producer revision/cutoff and later supported read; don't change a correct consumer mapping. |
| Daily eight-hour window | Daily recurrence is an uninterrupted eight-hour interval | Daily window resets; next 14:00 and 15h59 wait follow from supplied times. Execution and processing remain additional uncertainty. |
| VA/business daylight-saving clocks | All aggregation schedules shift with the browser/local zone | Fixed cluster offset gives 08:00 UTC and business 03:00 after offset changes. Verify actual runtime/clock basis. |
| Successful 90-of-100 scope | Higher frequency corrects an excluded population | Inspect approved scope/filter/actor visibility and deletion consequences before correction. Repeating a read doesn't expand its permitted/configured population. |
| New group details, older membership | Group-object freshness proves current account membership | Preserve separate observation times and obtain account-to-group evidence; group description alone proves no new membership observation. |
| Application read before provisioning | Calendar separation guarantees successful downstream delivery | 05:05 read correctly predates 05:12 target change. Required handoffs and later evidence, not merely clock ordering, establish outcome. |
| Current account, old Search | Stale indexed view necessarily means failed collection | Identify account/identity object, mapping output and synchronization evidence. No exact Search completion time is promised. |
| New upload, old export | Ingestion time equals live source verification | Export content/cutoff limits the evidence. Current input/import or a supported redesigned integration is required. |
| Thirty-minute contractor disable transfer | Hourly publication/collection and identity action meet target-disable deadline | Existing gaps already fail to support 30 minutes; define effective-time start, timely publication/collection, identity evaluation, authorized target action, completion confirmation, capacity/exceptions/escalation. Equivalent supported designs accepted only with those boundaries evidenced. |

Supplied facts, questions and worked reasoning remain in learner prose. No beginner comprehension or transfer test has occurred. Source checks and author review do not establish learner validation.

## AGG-007: Missing records, account deletion and safeguards

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 5.7.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Account loading/deletion settings/rounding/authoritative outcomes/hidden identities, Remove Account, profile priorities/mappings, correlation, JDBC SaaS delta actions and provisioning/lifecycle boundaries freshly checked.
- Author review: complete against teaching specification v2.6 for this scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. QueueDesk/WorkerRegistry, employee/account identifiers, populations, settings, canceled/corrected reads and all supplied target/identity results are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: source account-absence handling and identity consequences. No external or ISC account/identity is deleted. No threshold, deletion flag, source filter, permission, protected flag, elevated level, ownership or manager relationship is changed.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), source settings and Account Deletion Limitations | Enable Account Deletion permits removal of undetected ISC account representations; percentage setting cancels an aggregation with a warning at its documented limit behavior. Manual/scheduled runs share configured deletion settings; flat-file loading has deletion controls. Small-population rounding: less than one rounded account means cancellation only at 100%; ten-account 5% example rounds to one and cancels at one or more. No universal strict-greater-than comparison, internal denominator algorithm, atomic rollback or safe destructive preview is asserted. Main 150/1,000 is a supplied simple existing-population comparison clearly above 10%, not an inferred edge-boundary test. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), Deleting Authoritative Accounts and Identities / Blocking Deletion | Authoritative-account removal with deletion enabled leads to reassignment to another lower-priority authoritative profile with remapped values, or a nightly identity-deletion attempt if none remains. All listed blockers retained: other correlated accounts, access-definition/source ownership, active work-item owner/requester, manager references, elevated user level, protected flag, active campaign/certification. Hidden identities have no profile, no mapped lastname/email/uid and no normal Identities listing; previous attached accounts are treated as uncorrelated and grouped in relevant uncorrelated certifications. No immediate delete or target-access revocation guarantee. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), single-account section and history | UI Aggregate Account not-found retains the account. Source-level absence handling is not projected onto omitted delta records or every API. History status/start/stop/scanned/warning evidence supports investigation, not automatic approval or rollback assumptions. |
| [Managing accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html), Removing Accounts | Remove Account removes ISC representation, not the external record; later full aggregation can represent an external account again. Authoritative removal can change profile or Identities visibility. Recollection is not claimed to restore identical ISC IDs, history or all relationships. No procedural removal/reset recipe is supplied. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html), Prioritizing Authoritative Sources and mappings | One identity profile per identity; highest-priority eligible authoritative profile used. Fallback can change mapped values. Main QueueDesk is non-authoritative; separate WorkerRegistry account remains. No priority API or hidden-identity restoration command is executed. |
| [Assigning source accounts to identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Correlation is person/account matching and association. Existing correct qd-57007/wr-57007 associations are supplied, not inferred from identical employee digits. Recovery must review actual ownership rather than assume every new read restores all previous relationships. |
| [JDBC SaaS delta tables](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/jdbc/help/saas_connectivity/jdbc/delta_agg.html) | Supported account-key Delete action is an explicit connector-specific delta deletion signal, unlike omission of an unchanged account. Full-population absence and source UI safeguards are not declared universal for all connector/API paths. No SQL, tracking reset or database deletion is performed. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [Lifecycle states](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/lifecycle.html) | External account/access changes use configured supported provisioning and employment-state/access rules. A hidden identity is not a normal terminated lifecycle state. A source or identity representation disappearing does not establish external disablement, membership removal or session termination. Offboarding retention/status/end-date contract is a project decision, not a universal product retention mandate. |

### Example, arithmetic and diagram review

Devi 57007 coordinates delivery support. QueueDesk Production is a fictional non-authoritative source with 1,000 existing expected accounts, including 150 disabled ones; no new records or key changes in the comparison. qd-57007 is disabled but externally present and already correctly associated. Her identity remains established by separate WorkerRegistry account wr-57007.

An unintended active-only filter returns 850; all 150 omitted disabled accounts still exist. Candidate absence share is 150/1,000 = 15%, above the supplied 10% guard. Canceled warning and separately verified retained qd-57007 are supplied outcomes, not evidence of universal transactional rollback. An approved scope correction and supported full read include all 1,000 without target account enable/create/delete. Raising 10% to 20% is discussed as an inappropriate bypass, not executed.

Independent five-of-1,000 variation gives 0.5%, showing volume does not establish correctness. Ten-account 1% = 0.1 rounds to zero under the documented limitation; documented 5% = 0.5 rounds to one (10%), cancellation at one or more. Ordinary mathematical Python round(0.5) is not used to substitute banker's rounding for the explicit product example. Main comparison stays away from an ambiguous exact limit and does not infer undocumented general threshold arithmetic.

Other variations independently examine delta omission without Delete, remaining lower-priority authoritative account, no-authoritative/remaining-application hidden identity, protected emergency identity and Remove Account. Active-workers-only HR-file project is a transfer, not another defect in QueueDesk. Full-population file semantics and actual supported import behavior must be established before using absence.

Two text assets match inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization. Missing-population/guard diagram maximum width 37 characters; authoritative-account/identity diagram 45. First arrows are logical collection/guard progression with a separated human record check. Second branches summarize supported reassignment/nightly attempt/blocker outcomes; Delete allowed means eligibility, not completed deletion. Neither invents job internals, rollback, exact nightly completion or external target effects. Published rendering must be separately checked.

Two learner tables separate objects/states and illustrate whole-account rounding. All source citations, dates and assessment metadata stay outside learner prose.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning / evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Fifteen percent missing | Raising the guard repairs the integration | External existence and active-only exclusion establish a read-scope problem. Review actual state, approved correction and supported rerun; canceled status is not universal rollback evidence. |
| Five missing accounts | Below-limit removal must be correct | 0.5% quantity says nothing about external existence or approved scope. Identify records/actor/filter/completeness; small harmful omissions can pass a volume check. |
| Ten-account rounding | Lower percentage always gives stronger practical protection | 1% rounds to zero under the documented limitation; ten-account 5% case gives one and cancels at one or more. Do not generalize exact-edge rules or copy settings without source review. |
| Delta omission | Every absent account is a deletion candidate | Unchanged accounts can be omitted by design; supported explicit Delete evidence is distinct. |
| Another authoritative profile | Losing current HR account always deletes the person | Reassignment and recalculated profile values are documented; verify resulting profile/attributes and any separate access changes. |
| Other application account remains | Nightly deletion attempt confirms external removal | Remaining correlated account is a blocker; hidden identity and uncorrelated-account treatment do not remove target access. |
| Protected emergency identity | Protection is a defect to bypass for cleanup | Review approved protection/retention/emergency ownership decision; no safeguard removal merely to make a list clean. |
| Remove Account with live external record | Successful ISC removal should delete the external account | Represented account and external record are different targets. Recollection and separately authorized target change require distinct evidence. |
| Active-workers-only HR transfer | Feed omission and low threshold prove offboarding | Establish explicit status/end-date/retention and full/change semantics, profile/hidden/blocker consequences, identifiers, access rules, supported target operation/authority, safeguards and verification. Equivalent approved designs acceptable only with these distinctions. |

No beginner read-through or transfer validation has been performed. Source and author checks do not establish learner comprehension or formal approval.

## AGG-008: Reading aggregation outcomes

- Revision: 1.0. Course placement: Module 5.8.
- Documentation checked: October 21, 2026. Account activity/history/termination/modes/retries/Search/deletion-warning guidance, Web Services SaaS aggregation/paging, entitlement counts, correlation, identity-profile mappings and identity/provisioning boundaries freshly checked.
- Author review: complete against teaching specification v2.6 for this scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. WarehouseFlow, account/site data, Run A/B, pagination, connection error, counts and all post-run/recovery evidence are fictional supplied facts.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: interpreting execution and output evidence, not defining a universal UI/API counter schema or production retry recipe. No actual source, credential, permission, retry setting, delta state, account/identity or target change is made.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), Aggregation Activity / history / cancellation | Cross-source activity and source-specific history; status, start/stop, scanned accounts, optimization and terminated-task duration/unchanged information; status badge details and terminating actor. These are evidence for a specific execution, not universal scope or output correctness. No retention limit or five-row display limit is taught as necessary course content. Terminated work has data/evidence to inspect; no rollback guarantee is inferred. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html), methods / scheduling / errors / Search | Optimized unchanged-record skip includes account/manager correlation; unoptimized re-evaluation still needs delivered data. Queued scheduling is not completion. Supported retryable errors include connection reset; support/configuration and behavior are not universal. Deletion-volume safeguards can cancel with warning. Search is separately synchronized; no exact synchronization deadline is promised. |
| [Web Services SaaS aggregation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/web_services/help/saas_connectivity/web_services/json_aggregation.html) | Supported account operation, response extraction/mapping and paging context; operations can have endpoint dependencies. Main sequential 30/30/page-3-failure trace is a fictional configured design, not a universal connector execution architecture, pagination size, retry sequence or documented partial-write guarantee. |
| [Aggregating entitlements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/loading_entitlements/aggregating_entitlements.html) | Per-run discovered entitlement count can differ from total inventory accumulated through account and entitlement aggregations. All/selected supported types and prior collections matter; count mismatch does not prove duplicate objects or account memberships. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Person/account association and unchanged-record re-evaluation after correcting criteria. Main existing correct association is supplied. A high unchanged count does not prove criteria were reevaluated, and force-processing cannot repair missing read pages. |
| [Identity profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html), mappings | Account input and mapped identity values are different objects. No identity-site mapping or required identity change is invented for the main account-site incident. |
| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html), event-based processing | Identity evaluation can initiate external provisioning but does not validate successful target delivery. Evidence for account, identity and target outcomes remains distinct; no every-read/every-identity processing guarantee. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Supported external account/access changes are different from read-side representation. An account-update result is not automatically an external account modification; read membership is not a grant. |

### Main example, diagrams and arithmetic

Devi 58008 coordinates warehouse dispatches. WarehouseFlow Production uses a fictional supported Web Services SaaS read design; existing wf-58008 is correctly associated. Source site changes EAST to CENTRAL while the earlier ISC value is EAST. wf-58009 is a different received account whose new CENTRAL representation is independently checked.

Expected population is 100 specific existing accounts, all already represented; no create/delete/key changes in the two-run example. Pages are 30/30/30/10 under this supplied stable population. Run A starts 09:00, successfully receives two pages, fails retrieving page 3 and ends with Error at 09:08. No universal automatic retry count or pause is given. 30 + 30 = 60 unique accounts read; received-input comparison 50 same + 10 different = 60; 60 + 40 unread = 100 expected. Devi is among the 40 unread accounts.

The 50/10 comparison is teaching evidence, not a claim of exact UI/API counter labels, hash/comparison internals, or all ten verified account updates. Post-run inventory retains the same 100 expected identifiers. Post-run wf-58009 contains CENTRAL while wf-58008 remains EAST. The example explicitly excludes other operations changing ISC account values between Run A and its checks. These supplied observations refute universal no-change/rollback inference, not establish a general partial-write contract for every connector.

After resolved connection issue and reviewed settings/consequences, approved Run B starts 10:00 and succeeds at 10:06. All expected 100 distinct keys are supplied as obtained; at 10:07 wf-58008 CENTRAL is independently confirmed. Run durations are eight/six minutes, not performance guarantees. All times are UTC on one day. Main success requirement is population/account evidence only, not identity site or application-access change.

Independent variations include a successful 90-of-100 wrong scope; 95 unchanged of 100 optimized inputs after correlation change; one failure versus 40 unread; administrative termination; five discovered versus eight accumulated groups; 100 raw rows with 99 keys; and dashboard design. Main page size does not come from a universal default. No general duplicate overwrite/reject algorithm, retry resume, overlapping counter formula or transactional commit sequence is asserted.

Two text assets exactly match inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization, maximum widths 35/35 characters. First shows the supplied connector page progression and separates human post-run record checks. Second is an engineer's conditional evidence-check path, not an automated backend pipeline. Two tables distinguish evidence facts and count meanings. Published visual rendering must also be inspected.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning / evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Sixty scanned, hundred inventory | Stored inventory size proves fresh read coverage | Existing records can remain old; 40 required accounts were unread. Check identifiers and current values for the required population. |
| Earlier update after error | Overall error proves every prior change was rolled back | Actual wf-58009 state is evidence of one represented update; neither universal rollback nor all-ten success follows. |
| Success with ninety | Task success proves required business scope | Successful configured read can miss required accounts. Verify approved scope/keys/actor visibility and safe correction. |
| Ninety-five unchanged | Unchanged inputs were not read or all used corrected matching | Optimization can skip further correlation. Choose supported unchanged-record re-evaluation only when required; it does not recover unread input. |
| One failed page | Error event count equals affected-account count | Page evidence and expected set establish 40 unread records; preserve units and scope. |
| Administrator terminated | Stopped task proves failed authentication or need for credential replacement | Supplied terminating actor identifies a stop. Investigate why and actual progress/consequences; no credential change or rollback inferred. |
| Five discovered, eight inventory | Per-run versus inventory difference proves three duplicates | Review object IDs/types/scope/history and counter meaning; membership relationships are a separate quantity. |
| Hundred rows, ninety-nine keys | Raw count equals complete distinct population | Compare key sets and inspect connector-specific duplicate handling; no generic overwrite/rejection assumed. |
| Healthy dashboard transfer | Latest no-error task and familiar count prove every required outcome | Define source/object/mode/expected keys, freshness/cutoff, warning/status context, account values, required identity mapping/processing and actual external disablement. Retain unknown/incomplete states. Equivalent evidence-backed designs acceptable. |

Questions and supplied reasoning remain in learner prose. No beginner validation or transfer test occurred; author/source review and working-edition deployment do not substitute for those stages.

| [Processing identity data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Person-value evaluation uses profile/access configuration and is distinct from mere account read. Main correction requires reviewed supported collection/re-evaluation, not an invented private scheduling sequence. |
| [Provisioning overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | External account/access change differs from representation and ownership correction. Main needs no create/enable/delete or permission grant; read-side status repair is not external unlock/removal evidence. |

### Main trace, data and diagram review

Devi 59009 coordinates purchasing and already has an established ISC identity with employeeNumber text 59009. PurchaseDesk Production uses a fictional supported Web Services SaaS design. Existing external and represented account pd-59009 has login devi59009 and is uncorrelated. The required result is association with the existing person, not new identity/account creation.

Captured payload is explicitly the connector's actual production response for the compared read, not an external administrator's separate lookup. JSON contains data array with one selected record: accountKey pd-59009, login devi59009, nested person.number text 59009. Root $.data selects the account list. Schema accountKey identifies the account, login names it, employeeRef supplies the person-related input. Wrong mapping employeeRef <- login produces devi59009; required person.number produces text 59009.

One configured comparison is account employeeRef equals identity employeeNumber. Only one eligible identity has 59009, no identity has employee number devi59009, and no manual override applies. Thus wrong input fails the configured match despite valid read and account representation. No other operation changes compared records during diagnosis or correction checks.

Approved extraction correction preserves source, account key, login, criterion and existing identity. After reviewed supported collection/re-evaluation, supplied result represents employeeRef 59009, associates pd-59009 with Devi and is independently checked under her account view. No exact job order, optimizer hash behavior, universal mapping-triggered repair or particular mandatory force-refresh procedure is asserted. Shared mapping impact and source deletion settings require review.

Independent variations cover corrected criteria on unchanged input, unpublished batch, different actor visibility, missing page, HYD account versus BLR HR identity, stale Search and sandbox/production confusion. WorkOrders is an independent transfer: contract requires text code 0042; producer supplies text, project conversion emits numeric 42. Correct the evidenced conversion/type representation under review, not the producer's valid code or account ownership.

Selected JSON parses and uses quoted person number. Both mapping-table unchanged fields and the before/after comparison agree with the JSON and criterion. No credentials, live URLs or complete tenant API objects are embedded in learner prose.

Two editable text assets match inline blocks after trailing-whitespace normalization. Response-to-unresolved-ownership maximum line width 39; corrected-input/association maximum 38. First arrows are logical application/connector-input/correlation dependencies, not private transaction order. Second is a supplied before/after result with explicit eligibility assumptions, not a universal automatic action chain. Published visual rendering must be separately verified.

Two learner tables map observations to bounded conclusions and current/intended extraction. Technical qualifications and fictional assumptions remain in learner prose; official references, dates and assessment metadata stay here.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Case | Misconception tested | Accepted reasoning / evidence boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Source present, person absent | Missing under the person requires new application account | Record is represented and uncorrelated; inspect intended identity and actual match input/criteria instead of duplicating it. |
| Corrected criteria, unchanged input | New configuration means the unchanged record was evaluated | Optimized skip is distinct from executed comparison; use supported re-evaluation and review identity data/manual association. |
| Old published batch | Current business value proves consumer mapping failure | Connector receives old input; establish producer publication and later read before altering correct mappings. |
| Administrator sees, actor cannot | Administrator visibility proves connector visibility | Verify approved scope and least-privilege actor access, then actual response. An unavailable input is not extraction failure. |
| Required page missing | Force-processing can fix data never read | Resolve read coverage safely, preserve deletion safeguards and verify the delivered/represented record. |
| HYD account, BLR identity | All related location fields must match | Supplied meanings and HR identity mapping make both values correct. Do not rewrite authoritative input to match another business field. |
| Current records, old Search | Indexed discrepancy justifies source tracking reset | Inspect object/synchronization context; no exact Search deadline or arbitrary state reset. |
| Same name in sandbox | Another environment's match closes production issue | Establish production source and native key. A shared name is not object identity or read/association proof. |
| WorkOrders cost-centre transfer | Losing zeros requires producer edit or account recreation | Conversion is the evidenced divergence; preserve text 0042 under schema/contract, supported recollection, stable key/owner and reviewed affected dependencies. Equivalent evidence-backed correction accepted. |

No beginner read-through or independent transfer validation occurred. Source review, author review and deployment do not substitute for those stages.

### Fictional inputs and consistency review

The primary case uses Devi, logistics coordinator, employee 61001, PeopleLedger Production, authoritative Employees profile, workerKey hr-61001 and ISC uid worker61001. The JSON account maps workerNumber text 61001 to identificationNumber, familyName Rao to lastname, givenName Devi to firstname, workEmail devi61001@example.com to email and departmentCode LOG to department. HR provides the approved initial usable email. Profile/mappings are saved and appropriately applied; no existing person, conflicting username or other eligible profile. Successful person/association values are supplied fictional outcomes, not results observed in a tenant.

DispatchDesk variation uses existing independently created dd-61001 with employeeRef text 61001; non-authoritative source, sole comparison to identificationNumber, unique eligible person and no manual override. The highest-priority Employees example expressly assumes both authoritative accounts already belong to one identity. Independent contractor transfer uses ctr-C804, C804, contractorC804 and Sen, valid approved email and mappings, no existing identity or competing profile. These do not continue another lesson's biography.

Selected JSON, two diagrams and both tables were traced against prose and answers. Editable sources: [worker account to identity](../assets/IDN-001_worker_account_to_identity.txt), maximum width 40 characters; [existing identity and application account](../assets/IDN-001_existing_identity_and_application_account.txt), maximum width 41. First diagram describes data/configuration dependencies and a non-guarantee boundary, not a private execution sequence; second describes ISC ownership association, not a target write. Visual live rendering verification remains a separate publication check.

### Assessment reasoning map

| Entry | Understanding and supplied facts | Expected reasoning / acceptable evidence | Misconception exposed / source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | HR worker exists, approved integration view excludes pre-start workers, account not returned | Inspect intended timing/read scope and authorized owner before changes; identity mapping does not expand collection | Mapping repairs absent read input; loading data |
| Case 2 | Account present, familyName absent, direct lastname mapping and missing-value exception | Separate account representation from complete identity; source-owner correction, appropriate collection/processing and observed exception/value checks | Account presence proves complete onboarding; identity profiles |
| Case 3 | Two distinct workers, different account/person keys, same uid and duplicate exception | Naming/input defect, not proof of same person; maintain cross-profile uid uniqueness without changing valid account keys | Username collision requires merger; profiles/schema |
| Case 4 | Non-authoritative application account, no eligible identity with matching number | Investigate missing authoritative person/value; uncorrelated/shadow not profile-backed person creation | Every imported application account creates a personnel identity; correlation/sources |
| Case 5 | Same identity owns employee/contractor authoritative accounts, Employees higher priority and HR department mapping | Governing profile selects applicable mappings; no automatic department combination | Priority votes across fields or automatically merges separate people; profiles |
| Case 6 | Complete identity, absent application account, no supplied access/provisioning evidence | Investigate decision, support, request, attempt and result without assuming failed aggregation | Identity existence implies account/access creation; provisioning/processing |
| Case 7 | Work Email copies non-null not-an-email | Non-null requirement is not email-format validation, mailbox existence or delivery; inspect owning data and real address | No missing-value exception proves usable mail; profiles |
| Contractor transfer | Valid new-person account/profile with no existing identity/competition, no target outcome; changed null surname | Separate authoritative account, profile-backed identity, decision/fulfillment and target evidence; null surname redirects to required identity value path | Visible identity proves end-to-end onboarding or target fix resolves incomplete person; profiles/correlation/provisioning |

### Example and diagram review

Devi is independently reintroduced as a benefits coordinator, employee 62002, governed by Employees. PeopleCore Production is authoritative; hr-62002 supplies workerNumber 62002, iscUserName employee62002, familyName Rao, workDepartment HR and workerState active. Corporate Directory is non-authoritative with correctly associated cd-62002, department FIN and mail devi62002@example.com; it is also the configured supported authentication source. These roles are deliberately different.

Current identity Department mapping selects directory FIN. Approved changed mapping selects HR workDepartment; supplied applied result is identity HR with directory FIN unchanged and no attribute synchronization configured. No ownership/profile/credential change is implied.

Independent lifecycle variation assumes enabled provisioning and active state, valid saved/applied configuration, automatic assignment, one existing correctly associated bd-62002, supported entitlement operation/permissions and no competing assignments/removals. BenefitsDesk Production Benefits Self-Service access profile contains standing entitlement benefits-selfservice for employee self-service, not HR administration. Reported success and target membership are supplied fictional evidence, not tenant observations.

Both match inline blocks. Visual publication rendering is a separate check.

| [Inviting users](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/common/users/inviting_users.html) | Work Email registration invitations, profile/manual/lifecycle and certification-related triggers | Ordinary manual invitation setting is not represented as blocking every email path; no emails sent or protection configuration tested |
| [Roles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/roles.html) | Configured role membership can consume identity values | Selecting ENG does not itself prove a particular role or external permission is assigned |

Review emphasized profile mapping/required-field sections, First Valid overview/properties/examples, Account Attribute selection, attribute sync direction and safeguards, processing's manual/event-based sections and invitation trigger descriptions. Official sites currently use Human Fabric/Identity Services/Identity Security branding; the ISC course terminology is retained without mixing IdentityIQ behavior.

### Fictional policy and example consistency

Devi is independently introduced as an employee transferring Operations to Engineering, number 64004. PersonnelHub Production is authoritative and Employees governs the one correctly associated identity. PersonnelHub ph-64004 homeDepartment ENG is HR-approved effective October 21, 2026 at 09:00 UTC and collected 09:05. Corporate Directory cd-64004 department OPS is an older downstream copy; its record was modified for a phone correction 09:08 and collected 09:10. ProjectFlow Production pf-64004 department PRJ-17 is a project resource group, collected 09:11, not employment Department.

CostLedger Production fc-64004 allocationCostCenter text 0042, collected 08:45, is Finance's approved current allocation. Additional agreed inputs are PersonnelHub workerNumber 64004, iscUserName employee64004, familyName Rao, and Corporate Directory mail devi64004@example.com. These are selected fictional mappings; systems and teams do not imply universal SailPoint ownership rules. Initial identity Department OPS selects the directory; supplied approved/applied mapping correction selects PersonnelHub and yields ENG, with directory OPS unchanged and no synchronization write configured.

The required-email cycle expressly assumes this project's account-creation path needs an established identity and the required email mapping depends on that future account, with no initial alternative. It is not universal ISC architecture. Supported solutions are discussed as design options requiring actual requirements and acceptance checks, including a deliberate technical placeholder versus usable contact data and controlled consumers.

Independent variations include contractor coverage gaps, first candidate empty text, first input calculation error/default ignoreErrors false, none with unspecified safeguards, receiving-copy feedback and current OPS/next ENG effective November 1. Transfer has sponsor-approved November 30 versus unapproved proposed December 31 application expiry, with no authority for an extension. No access or business date is changed.

### Diagram and narration review

- [Approved department input](../assets/IDN-004_approved_department_input.txt): synchronized with inline source, maximum width 45 characters. Arrow is configured data dependency, not source voting, transaction sequence or target synchronization.
- [Required email dependency](../assets/IDN-004_required_email_dependency.txt): synchronized, maximum width 44. Arrows mean depends on under the supplied project constraints; not a retry loop or mandatory ISC backend design.
- Two two-column tables retain full candidate evidence and field-level selected inputs/reasons for narrow-screen readability. Supporting source/owner/producer/time/mapping/transform/processing/provisioning/sync/fallback/invitation/role/lifecycle terms are introduced before process dependence.
- Narration and humanizer pass checks independent context, causal reasoning, useful qualifications and no teaching-method announcements, external learner citations, false tenant-test claims or confidence promises. Visual publication checks remain separate.

### Assessment reasoning map

| Entry | Understanding / supplied facts | Expected reasoning and acceptable evidence | Misconception / reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Case 1 | HR ENG effective/read before later directory OPS; HR owns field | Distinguish approved field fact from collection recency; inspect mapping and receiving-copy correction separately | Most recent read automatically wins; profiles/loading |
| Case 2 | Project resource grouping PRJ-17 versus employment Department | Establish business meaning and separate needed attributes; label match insufficient | Same label means same field semantics; supplied agreement/profiles |
| Case 3 | Complete employees but no contractor CostLedger accounts, no alternate policy | Coverage and approved alternate/exception design; one preview insufficient | Employee sample certifies all populations; profiles/account lookup |
| Case 4 | First candidate actual empty string, later real address, no rejection/normalization | Empty text non-null under documented condition; business validation still required | firstValid proves usable email; First Valid/profiles |
| Case 5 | First calculation errors, default ignoreErrors false, later non-null | Error is not ignored merely because later input exists; investigate defect and explicit policy | All errors automatically trigger safe fallback; First Valid |
| Case 6 | Deliberate none satisfies non-null, no consumer/address/delivery evidence | Required-value success is not registration-mail readiness; identify missing safeguards/transition evidence | Placeholder is a usable mailbox or manual setting controls all triggers; profiles/First Valid/invitations |
| Case 7 | Upstream ENG, identity from downstream OPS, proposed write-back to same account | Receiving copy cannot supply missing approved fact; review origin and independently verify writes | Sync configuration establishes business authority; attr sync |
| Case 8 | Current OPS, next ENG effective November 1, observation October 21 | Respect current meaning/date/time-zone and approved selection; publication not effectivity | Future published record applies immediately; supplied policy/profile mapping |
| Sponsor-date transfer | Approved November 30, unapproved proposed December 31 application copy, newer read | Sponsor approval governs intended identity fact; investigate unapproved application value and authorized corrections; verify consumer boundary semantics separately | Later date/newer observation approves extension or matching screens justify expanded access; supplied authority/sync/processing |
