## IDN-005

### Revision, checks and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 6.5, **Two worker records. Which identity profile governs the person?** Documentation checked October 21, 2026. This is documentation-based author review, not tenant execution, observed beginner comprehension or formal publication approval. Existing lessons and the attribute tracing guide revision 1.0 are unchanged.

The independent fictional example uses employee 65005; approved stable person reference P65005; teaching identity label I-65005, not an ISC object ID; PeopleCore Production h-65005, ContractRegister Production c-65005 and Corporate Directory d-65005. Both worker accounts are already correctly associated through the supplied person-reference correlation. No account association, source, priority, profile, identity, lifecycle state, deletion or target is changed in a tenant.

Main profile configuration is Employees 10, Contractors 20, Subsidiary Staff 5. The last profile has no authoritative account on this identity and does not compete. Employees maps Department from PeopleCore departmentCode ENG and custom Worker Type from workerCategory EMPLOYEE; Contractors uses assignmentDepartment OPS and workerCategory CONTRACTOR. Both map email from directory mail devi65005@example.com and produce unique uid person65005. Required values are assumed available. Successful processing is supplied fictional evidence, not a measured platform execution.

The reversed creation-order case is an independent configuration: Contractors 10, Employees 20; proposed Employees 5 illustrates a shared change, not a per-person API command. Case 4 deliberately separates the accounts across identities A and B. Case 7 explicitly supplies confirmed authoritative-account deletion through the approved aggregation path, a remaining associated contractor account and successful required mappings. The acquisition transfer is a new design problem, not Devi's continuing biography.

### Official sources and claim mapping

Earlier-created defaults 10 then 20. Per-profile mappings can read other sources; lower profile is not an implicit field fallback. Duplicate uid generates an exception, not a merge instruction. |
| [Official Identity Profile schema](https://github.com/sailpoint-oss/api-specs/blob/main/idn/apis/identity-profiles/schemas/identityprofile.yaml) | priority numeric field, authoritativeSource reference, identityAttributeConfig, identityCount and identityRefreshRequired | Configuration object roles corroborated directly from maintained vendor API specification; no runnable request, historical endpoint guarantee or automatic reassignment timing asserted. |

No fixed delay or claim that one profile's current membership covers every priority-change impact. Processing does not validate successful provisioning delivery. |

No assertion that a priority change removes every old permission or changes every session/login. |

Missing row, inactive status, transform filter and actual removal remain different. Documented behavior is not a recommendation to delete records to force conversion. |

The current documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places. The course retains the requested ISC name; this does not establish a licensing entitlement or feature availability in every tenant.

### Engineering interpretation and excluded claims

- Eligible profile and governing profile are teaching shorthand explained in the learner body. They are not additional product fields or a claim about private implementation.
- Population boundaries are an approved integration design. Profile names and job-title strings do not automatically implement those boundaries.
- Priority is shared precedence, not data-quality scoring, most-recent-record selection, arbitrary per-identity exceptions or a global source ranking for every attribute.
- No tied-priority outcome is taught. Distinct intended priorities are used; a production change must follow the currently supported specification and approved scope.
- A static ordering cannot encode contradictory outcomes for two identities with the same eligible-profile pattern. This is reasoning from the documented condition, not a separate product limitation inferred from a forum.
- The lesson does not claim that merely saving priority immediately reassigns all identities, that one specific manual-processing action is a universal reassignment recipe, or that lower-priority inactive accounts automatically disappear.
- Supported account-deletion reassignment is described only under its documented conditions. If more than one account exists on the governing source, do not infer that deleting one necessarily removes every qualifying association; the supplied case has only h-65005 on that source.
- An unexpected access result is investigated through assignment/removal and fulfillment evidence. No universal removal, rollback, session termination or mailbox/username change is promised.
- All examples describe supplied facts or conditional design reasoning; none is advertised as lab/production observation.

### Diagrams and presentation checks

- [Profile eligibility and priority](../assets/IDN-005_profile_eligibility_and_priority.txt), maximum 43 characters: two attached authoritative accounts, one absent higher-ranked profile, then ranking among eligible profiles.
- [Contractor-to-employee transition](../assets/IDN-005_contractor_to_employee_transition.txt), maximum 39 characters: same identity before/after new associated authoritative account, supplied successful Employees mapping results and separate human verification.
- Both editable assets match their inline text blocks. Nearby prose explains conditions, arrows, unshown association mechanics and the difference between logical dependency and an internal job timeline.
- Two two-column tables compare configured populations/priorities and selected per-profile mappings. Full source text, diagrams, tables, all forty-three routes, navigation and the unchanged downloadable tracing aid are publication checks; visual rendering is checked separately on the live page.
- No external links, course navigation links or source-citation labels are added to the learner article. Author metadata stays here.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Highest-priority profile absent | Eligibility precedes rank | Only Contractors has an authoritative account on this identity, so it governs despite a lower-priority number elsewhere | Lowest number anywhere governs everyone | Profile priority |
| 2. Lower-ranked record read later | Observation time versus configured precedence | Employees 10 governs the supplied same identity with both accounts; inspect its mappings for disputed values | Last aggregation wins | Profile priority; processing |
| 3. Correct profile, wrong input | Profile selection versus per-field source | Repair approved Department mapping and verify applied result; changing already-winning priority cannot change its input | Governing profile forces every value from its authoritative source | Profile mappings; Preview/Apply |
| 4. Same name, separate identities | Ownership and continuity versus selection | Inspect stable references, history and correlation; priority doesn't merge A/B or authorize deletion | Display-name match or priority combines identities | Correlation; profile association |
| 5. Defaults contradict policy | Shared precedence and population risk | Correct ordering through supported approved change; assess all overlaps, old/new mappings/settings, processing and observed results | Creation order is business policy or one sample proves all | Profiles; update specification; processing |
| 6. Inactive status, attached account | Account data versus relationship | No evidence that account presence/order changed; inspect actual profile and lifecycle logic | Inactive always transfers to lower profile | Profile condition; lifecycle; deletion |
| 7. Confirmed authoritative removal | Conditional fallback/recalculation | Contractors governs the remaining associated authoritative account under supplied successful mapping assumptions; inspect identity and access separately | Missing export equals confirmed deletion or reassignment proves delivery | Authoritative-account deletion; processing |
| 8. Profile changed, group remains | Identity decision versus target outcome | Inspect assignment basis, removal requirements, initiated work and target result; group alone does not locate defect | Profile selection proves all old access removed | Lifecycle; processing delivery boundary |
| Acquisition transfer / teach-back | Apply complete reasoning to new population | Establish same-identity associations, stable references, intended overlap policy and effective date handling, profiles/consumers, processing and separate outcome evidence; question static priority if migration timing requires a distinction it cannot encode alone | HR label or acquisition date automatically determines profile and access | Profile condition/mappings; correlation; processing |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. The observed learner-validation result remains pending.

## IDN-006

### Revision, review and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 6.6, **The account arrived. Why is the identity incomplete?** Documentation checked October 21, 2026. Documentation-based author review only; no tenant execution, observed beginner validation or formal publication approval. Prior lessons, presentation behavior and the complete attribute tracing aid revision 1.0 are unchanged.

Independent fictional example: joining employee Devi 66006; WorkforceBook Production authoritative source; Employees governing profile; account wb-66006. The selected JSON extract contains workerNumber text 66006, loginName person66006, workMail devi66006@example.com, familyName Rao and surname null. Both surname and familyName remain defined in the supplied account schema; surname is retired. The initial Last Name mapping reads surname. The supplied incomplete identity has the approved uid/email but no lastname; profile exception evidence identifies that missing required value. Account ownership and uid uniqueness are assumed verified.

The approved correction selects familyName, and successful processing produces lastname Rao on the same intended identity while uid/email remain correct. Current exception evidence clears and the identity no longer appears in Incomplete Identities. These are fictional supplied observations, not executed UI/API/account/profile changes or exact replicas of report fields. The source surname is already correct; no real HR data, naming rule, profile, authentication, identity, correlation, deletion, provisioning or external target operation occurs.

### Official references and claim mapping

| Source | Sections and behavior checked | Lesson boundary |
|---|---|---|
| [Creating Identity Profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Required uid/email/lastname mappings and non-null outputs, cross-profile username uniqueness, Work Email not format-validated, cross-source mappings, Preview, Identity Exceptions download and source-data correction | Configured mapping is different from successful per-person output. Duplicate uid is an exception, not an automatic merge. Main mapping correction is engineering reasoning over supplied inputs, not a claim that every exception originates in HR. |
| [Working with Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/identity_mgmt.html) | All/Incomplete/Errors filters, incomplete sign-in/request/certification restrictions, statuses and error-detail examples for alternate email and authentication-source mismatch | Incomplete is not all errors. An available required email does not establish that a separately configured alternate invitation address exists. The lesson does not equate account/identity/lifecycle states or teach destructive reset/deletion as repair. |
| [Processing Identity Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Event-based updates, manual application of profile configuration, Preview/Apply scope, applied identity evidence, Search synchronization and independent provisioning verification | Processing existing data does not itself reread the external producer. Preview/save/initiation are distinct from applied results. No fixed completion/index delay, universal automatic retry or proof of provisioning success is promised. |
| [Loading Account Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Source aggregation, activity/history, source read scope and view freshness | Correction needs the actual account read and changed represented data, not green aggregate status alone. Omitted input is not assumed to universally clear existing data; no target change follows merely from a successful read. |
| [First Valid transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/first-valid/) | Ordered first non-null result, static strings/nested results, ignoreErrors default false, documented email placeholder example | Empty text differs from null; a literal none/unknown is not business approval or a usable mailbox. Explicit error handling differs from null fallback. Transform evaluation is separated from connector normalization and required-field validation. |
| [Account Attribute transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/) | Lookup uses associated identity accounts for a selected source and account field | Supports selected-account input inspection, not a universal person-name lookup, arbitrary read of another identity's account or every-operation account-selection algorithm. |
| [Assigning Source Accounts to Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured comparisons establish account-to-identity associations; unmatched accounts remain uncorrelated | Ownership investigation for a repeated-person collision is separate from naming uniqueness for different people. No forced merge or destructive correction recipe is inferred. |

Current official documents use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places. The course retains the requested ISC title; source terminology does not assert a licensing entitlement or availability in every tenant.

### Reasoning and deliberately excluded claims

- The two required-value checks are shared configured mappings and actual per-identity results. A valid schema field, a saved mapping, a successful read or a sample preview does not alone prove that a particular required output exists.
- The main selected account explicitly contains null in the retired field. Empty, whitespace, omitted and literal-null examples are changed conditions; the lesson does not assert undocumented acceptance/trim/clearing behavior across all connectors, transforms and platform validation stages.
- Incomplete Identities and Identity Exceptions share relevant missing-required evidence but are not a universal one-to-one categorization of every duplicate or processing error. Read the specific affected record and error detail.
- A required non-null email check does not validate address syntax, mailbox existence, ownership, delivery or all invitation settings. The contractor transfer requires an approved dependency/consumer-control design rather than recommending placeholders by default.
- Different source account IDs can feed the same calculated uid. Cross-profile uniqueness is applied to the output, not assumed from distinct raw records.
- Collision investigation distinguishes same-person continuity from different-person naming; matching usernames do not authorize merging, changing an unrelated person's identifier or deleting an identity.
- Missing manager/department/end date may break a project dependency without meeting the missing-required-attribute definition. No universal fail-closed access behavior, blanket provisioning freeze or external account deletion is inferred from incompleteness.
- Applied identity repair and current exception/status evidence must be checked for the specific identity and other affected records. Historical reports/counts and stale Search observations do not independently prove present correctness or failure.
- Source collection, identity processing and external provisioning remain separate operations. Source-data correction, integration correction and mapping correction require different approved actions; the map is human engineering reasoning, not an automated repair feature.

### Diagrams and publication checks

- [Missing required value trace](../assets/IDN-006_missing_required_value_trace.txt), maximum 39 characters: collected familyName Rao/surname null, wrong profile input, missing identity lastname and supplied incomplete/exception evidence.
- [Cause-based correction](../assets/IDN-006_cause_based_correction.txt), maximum 43 characters: human diagnosis branches to producer, collection/extraction or mapping fixes; collection/processing dependencies converge on specific-identity and separate downstream checks.
- Both editable assets match the inline blocks. Prose explains actor, conditions, arrow meaning, omitted implementation and that evidence displays need not update synchronously.
- One selected JSON extract is syntactically valid. Two two-column tables explain required values and input-state distinctions. Full source text, JSON, diagrams, tables, all forty-four routes, navigation and unchanged aid text are publication checks; live visual rendering is checked separately.
- Learner article contains no external source links, course links or reference labels. Assessment metadata stays outside the teaching body.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Correct source, wrong input | Locate the defective boundary | familyName Rao is already collected; mapping surname null explains missing output; correct approved profile mapping and validate scope | Every missing identity value requires HR correction | Profiles/mappings; supplied JSON |
| 2. Correction not collected | Aggregation versus processing | Existing account is stale; publish/read the corrected record, inspect represented result and reevaluated identity | Process Identity rereads HR | Aggregation; processing |
| 3. Empty first result | Exact null semantics | First successful empty result is non-null; inspect actual output and approved normalization, not assumed fallback | First Valid means first usable or non-empty value | First Valid |
| 4. Failed calculation | Error versus null | Default ignoreErrors false does not silently skip failed evaluation; fix cause or justify explicit handling | All errors behave like missing values | First Valid |
| 5. Collision across profiles | Global uniqueness and person distinction | Confirmed different people need distinct approved uid outputs; profile naming does not isolate usernames | Each profile has its own username namespace | Required attributes; exceptions |
| 6. Complete values, authentication error | Specific error interpretation | Missing account relationship/authentication configuration is separate from Last Name; investigate supported association/configuration | Every identity error is incomplete required data | Identity error details; correlation |
| 7. Missing manager | Technical completeness versus consumer dependency | Inspect manager input/relationship and approval policy; no inferred universal fail-safe or invented manager | No Incomplete flag means every workflow can operate correctly | Profiles/manager dependency; supplied scenario |
| 8. Cleared exception, retained group | Identity repair versus external outcome | Required values and current evidence support repair; inspect separate removal policy/process/target state | Cleared exception proves all downstream delivery | Processing delivery boundary |
| Contractor intake transfer / teach-back | Apply full trace to changed dependency | Establish approved initial/final email inputs, consumer controls and verified transition; distinguish null, empty, error, collision and uncollected repair; identify owner and evidence | Populated placeholder and green count establish a complete safe onboarding | Profiles; First Valid; aggregation; processing |

Learner questions and worked answers remain in the lesson. No beginner read-through or transfer-test result is claimed.

Directory attribute sync is explicitly enabled for department with an eligible direct identity Department mapping in its Create Account definition.

The coordinated example supplies approved ENG consumer logic and approved target code format, identity ENG and correct role, unchanged manager/lifecycle active, but a denied directory write and actual directory Engineering. Administration account cd-admin-67007 appears only in the changed multi-account case; OPS supplies a negative role test. ### Official sources and claim mapping

The initially attempted monitoring.html URL is not current (404); the official navigation led to tracking.html, which was read and is the reference retained here. Current documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places; the course keeps the requested ISC title without implying licensing/feature availability for every tenant.

### Engineering reasoning and excluded guarantees

- Main department change is a representation migration, not a job transfer. Identical business meaning does not make old/new text comparisons equivalent or approve the receiving format in every project.
- Dependency review follows actual inputs, referenced calculations, consumers and writers. It does not claim every mapping change triggers every manager/lifecycle/role/integration branch.
- Profile mappings are shared. Preview and selected-person processing are not a private canary deployment. A limited real rollout needs supported approved scope and coordination with other event/scheduled processing.
- A transition can temporarily contain old values with new criteria or the converse. Dual-value acceptance is only a conditional design option requiring population approval, not a default product feature or blanket safe rollout recipe.
- Required field presence is not full business validity. Missing/future inputs and multiple-account cases test assumptions; the sample table is not a record of tenant validation.
- The null write and all-correlated-account behavior are specifically attributed to configured attribute sync. They are not borrowed from provisioning account selection or applied to sources with no eligible sync path.
- A correct identity value can coexist with target failure. Repair permission/delivery at the failing boundary rather than altering authoritative business facts to make a stale receiving copy appear consistent.
- The successful-sync rollback variation requires both target change and collection of changed data. That condition is separate from the supplied denied-write outcome.
- Configuration restoration is not reversal of changed input, completed provisioning, notifications or intervening legitimate business updates. Compensating recovery is new approved action; no universal rollback, cancellation, harmless retry or deleted-account/history restoration is asserted.
- User Name, Work Email and Last Name remain required; additional manager/lifecycle/consumer correctness and actual target state need their own evidence.
- No live changes, connector permission grants, rollback commands, deletions or production rollout are executed.

### Diagrams and presentation checks

- [Department consumers](../assets/IDN-007_department_consumers.txt), maximum 44 characters: changed identity code branches to an unchanged role comparison and separately configured directory sync, followed by human checks.
- [Restoring a mapping](../assets/IDN-007_restoring_a_mapping.txt), maximum 41 characters: old directory label path; a separate successful new-code sync and collection; restored old mapping now reading changed input; human recovery.

## IDN-008

### Revision, review and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 6.8, **Where did this identity value come from?** Documentation checked October 21, 2026. Documentation-based author review only; no tenant execution, observed beginner validation or formal publication approval. Earlier lessons, site behavior and tracing guide revision 1.0 are unchanged. All eight Module 6 lessons are now authored, which does not establish completion of learner validation or the whole course.

Independent fictional example: employee Devi 68008, teaching identity label I-68008 (not a real object ID), StaffLedger Production authoritative for Employees, account sl-68008 approved costCenter text 0042 effective 09:00 and collected 09:05. Corporate Directory cd-68008 has text with surrounding spaces 0900, collected 09:08. Current identity costCenter 0900 is observed 09:10, mapping selections 09:12, current Preview 0900 at 09:13. All times are October 21, 2026 UTC. Finance approves the StaffLedger field as current cost-centre input; no future proposal is used.

Both supplied accounts belong to the same intended identity, with exactly one relevant account per source. UI selections name StaffLedger/costCenter, but Trim Cost Center explicitly nests accountAttribute with sourceName Corporate Directory and attributeName costCenter. The selected directory text trims to 0900. Correcting the actual explicit source to StaffLedger Production yields Preview and supplied post-processing identity text 0042, preserving zeros. No directory cost-centre synchronization is configured, so no directory write is expected. All configuration, observations and successful correction are supplied fictional evidence, not real system executions or screenshots of tenant records.

Priority is not field fallback or a way to merge identities. One calculated preview differs from applied identity/target outcomes. |

Trim receives directory text, not HR 0042; no numerical conversion or arbitrary replacement is inferred. |

Changed two-account case uses documented oldest-created default with a non-null first value. No universal newest/oldest ISC selection rule or ownership repair is implied. |

Source labels/display names alone do not establish the input path. No claim of tenant-tested reference resolution. |

Saved correction, initiated/completed processing, current identity and consumer outcomes need distinct evidence. |

Work-email transfer requires proving the actual consumer/write path before classifying an application discrepancy. |

Documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places; the requested ISC course title is retained without implying every licensed feature or connector is present.

### Trace assumptions, engineering reasoning and excluded claims

- HR expected value is an approved current business fact in the supplied example. The integration is not choosing authority from recency or the familiar HR label.
- The selected JSON is a valid reading example, not an API request or command to upload into a tenant. Corrected explicit input is described in prose, preserving the intended operation and field.
- Current source values plus current saved definition explain current Preview 0900 and are consistent with the observed identity value. They do not prove the exact earlier execution that created it.
- Historical causality requires the earlier revision, inputs and execution context. The lesson explicitly permits an incomplete historical conclusion rather than fabricating backend timing or private queue behavior.
- Source field absent/wrong/type-changed, wrong account/profile, unexpected explicit input, unapplied correction and stale view are separate hypotheses. Correcting one does not repair every other boundary.
- Leading-zero cost-centre values remain strings. Numeric 42 does not prove an original identifier of 0042; the correct width/meaning requires the business contract.
- AccountAttribute defaults apply to that lookup. Filtering that lookup does not remove an account or redefine connector collection scope.
- The main correction changes an explicit supplier, not HR data, account ownership or profile priority. Shared transform references and affected consumers must be reviewed before applying it.
- Preview is not persistence and Apply Changes is not source aggregation. A correct identity can coexist with missing/failed external writes. No universal retry, atomic rollout, target rollback or automatic email update is promised.
- Inline eight-question trace applies the same evidence dimensions as the unchanged complete guide revision 1.0. It is a human investigation order, not a backend execution sequence.
- All records, times, calculations and before/after outcomes are illustrative. No source/profile/transform/account/correlation/identity/sync or target change is performed.

- [Correction and verification](../assets/IDN-008_correction_and_verification.txt), maximum 41 characters: current wrong input, approved input correction and selected Preview, separately supplied post-processing identity, unchanged directory and human checks.
- Both editable assets match the inline blocks. Prose identifies arrow meaning, conditions, actor, current-versus-historical limits and that preview does not automatically apply output.
- The selected nested JSON parses; a local illustrative model checks space trimming, preserved 0042, selected source and the approved source change. This is source-example validation, not execution of ISC transforms.
- Two two-column tables contain evidence/times and competing hypotheses. Full source text, JSON, diagrams, tables, all forty-six lesson routes/navigation and exact unchanged downloadable aid text are publication checks. Visual rendering is checked separately on the live page.
- All six navigation modules remain; do not create a seventh module until COR-001 exists. The next reserved lesson is Module 7.1, not authored in this change.
- No external links, course links or source-reference labels are in the learner article.

| 1. UI selection versus explicit input | Actual calculation provenance | Inspect nested directory lookup; unchanged explicit source ignores reliance on selected HR input | Selected source label alone proves supplier | Trim; Account Attribute |

Correct identity, old Search | Current record versus view | Compare exact identity/scope/time and synchronization evidence; don't rewrite good mapping prematurely | One old view disproves current identity record or all discrepancies are automatically delay | Processing; aggregation view notes |
| 7. Present value reproduces past output | Historical causality | Missing historical revision/input prevents proving exact prior execution; state limit | Same output proves identical historical path | Supplied evidence limits; current definition behavior |

| Work-email transfer / teach-back | Apply evidence trace to changed object | Identify identity/account/message/application observation, exact owner and mapping, preview/applied/write evidence; test same-name/absent-input/reference/report variations | All email complaints are one identity-mapping defect | Profiles; Account Attribute; Reference; correlation; processing; sync |

## COR-001

### Revision, review and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 7.1, **The account was imported. Why isn't it on the person's identity?** Documentation checked October 21, 2026. Documentation-based author review only; no tenant execution, observed beginner validation or formal publication approval. All previous lessons, the tracing guide revision 1.0 and presentation code are unchanged. A seventh navigation module is added using existing manifest-driven behavior.

Independent fictional example: employee Devi 71001; PeopleBook Production authoritative for Employees, worker account p-71001 already attached to teaching identity I-71001 (not an actual object ID). Identity identificationNumber is text 71001, identity name worker71001, display name Devi Rao. LedgerPay Production is non-authoritative for employee establishment. Account ID designation accountKey contains lp-87; Account Name designation loginName contains drao; friendly displayName is Devi Rao; employeeRef is initially null.

Only configured pairing is LedgerPay employeeRef to identity identificationNumber. No other configured comparison matches, and no identity has the name drao, so the documented default also provides no match. Initial uncorrelated result is supplied. Owner-approved feed correction supplies employeeRef text 71001; collection and reevaluation are supplied, the target reference is unique, and the same account becomes associated with the same intended identity. Login/account key remain unchanged. No sync or access-changing operation is configured as part of the correction. No actual source/profile/correlation/account/identity/reset/deletion/sync or target change occurs.

### Official references and claim mapping

| Source | Sections or claims checked | Lesson boundary |
|---|---|---|
| [Assigning Source Accounts to Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Configured account/identity comparisons, prerequisites, preset and default logic, default Account Name to identity name case-insensitive comparison, uncorrelated reports, unchanged account reexamination, manual correlation permanence and ISC-created account treatment | Missing configured reference alone is not universal proof of uncorrelated status. Main case explicitly excludes a default match. Display name differs from schema-designated Account Name and identity name. No destructive reset/removal or automatic manual-link reassignment recipe is taught. |
| [Managing Accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Human/machine/uncorrelated classifications, account status/attributes, source account correlation management and governance requirement | Uncorrelated means missing authoritative identity relationship, not external deletion or absence of business ownership. Shared/machine accountability must use appropriate classification; no license entitlement or personal correlation for every service account is implied. |
| [Creating Identity Profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative source/profile identity establishment, identity attributes and cross-source mappings | Existing employee identity is already established. Making LedgerPay authoritative would change population establishment rather than prove ownership. Correlation, profile selection and identity field mappings are distinct responsibilities. |
| [Loading Account Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Account reads/representation, aggregation evidence and optimization context, view freshness | Account existence in the target, source representation and owner association are separate observations. Logical teaching stages are not separate mandatory jobs. Correcting feed data must reach ISC and be evaluated; success of one read is not full outcome proof. |
| [Viewing Identity Control Panels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | Identity profile/details and access/account relationships, represented access evidence | Person-level view describes associated data; source inventory and current matching evidence are needed when account is absent from that person's view. Matching UI names or a correlation flag do not establish business ownership. |
| [Managing Sources: Assigning a Source Owner](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html#assigning-a-source-owner) | Source Owner manual fulfillment and certification responsibilities | Operational owner of a source is not automatically the personal owner/user of all its accounts. The 120-account bulk-assignment proposal is rejected on that distinction, not a blanket prohibition of supported manual correlation. |
| [Processing Identity Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Identity evaluation and role/lifecycle/manager/access requirements; provisioning delivery limit | Correct association can affect data available to configured consumers, but does not prove every role/lifecycle change or successful external delivery. Main example supplies no access-changing work. |
| [Synchronizing Attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html) | Existing correlated accounts and association changes as possible triggers for configured sync | Moving/correlating an account can matter beyond a display label when sync exists. Main correction explicitly has no sync write; no universal automatic field update or access grant is inferred. |

The initially attempted account_mgmt.html path was not current; official navigation identifies accounts/index.html as Managing Accounts, which was read and retained. Current documents use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places; the course retains the requested ISC title without claiming feature licensing in every tenant.

### Engineering assumptions and excluded claims

- Account recognition within a source and assigning the account to a person are different relationships. The account's key lp-87, login drao and employee reference 71001 need not be identical.
- Default correlation is not ignored. Its documented comparison uses schema-selected Account Name and identity name, not every friendly/full-name field. The case-insensitive rule is named but no unverified tie-breaking or ambiguous-candidate algorithm is invented.
- A successful comparison and recorded association must be checked against approved ownership evidence. Wrong data can produce a correlated account with the wrong person.
- Uncorrelated is a technical relationship state. Orphan is presented as an organization-defined business-risk description, not an official universal ISC state or automatic synonym.
- No disable/delete/removal is supplied by a failed match. Existing external login/permissions can coexist with uncorrelated status; actual source access requires its own evidence.
- The main correction is a changed matching input, not a configuration or manual-link change. The figure explicitly assumes supported reevaluation and unique approved target; no fixed processing or screen-update delay is promised.
- Unoptimized aggregation after changed correlation configuration is distinct from clearing manual association. The explanation preserves documented persistent manual and ISC-created-account treatment.
- Machine/service/shared examples introduce account-type and responsible-person distinctions only. The lesson does not teach machine-product configuration, license promises or personal-user assignment for all service accounts.
- Manual correlation can be an approved exception, but broad defects require cause-based correction. Source reset, account removal, manual move and identity deletion are not executed or recommended as count cleanup.
- Main association correction does not change login, account ID, employee identity or permissions. Any real downstream dependency needs separate reviewed execution and target evidence.

### Diagrams and presentation checks

- [Collection and relationship](../assets/COR-001_collection_and_relationship.txt), maximum 45 characters: established PeopleBook relationship, collected LedgerPay record, separate configured/default no-match evidence and human ownership check.
- [Verified relationship correction](../assets/COR-001_verified_relationship_correction.txt), maximum 47 characters: same account before/after approved employeeRef data correction, reevaluation and association to the same identity.
- Both editable assets match inline blocks. Prose identifies conditions, arrows, actor, that aggregation can include collection/correlation in one run, and that diagrams do not describe private internal services.
- Two two-column tables hold supplied records/configuration and five observation boundaries. Eight worked cases plus the project transfer address the principal distinctions. Complete live text, diagrams, tables, all forty-seven routes and seven-module native defaults/current-page links are publication checks; visual rendering is checked separately.
- Attribute tracing aid revision 1.0 is unchanged. COR-002 is reserved but not authored. Source links and assessment metadata remain outside the learner article.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Imported but no reference | Collection versus association | Supplied no configured/default match means no person relationship despite successful import; inspect owner/matching input | Successful read identifies owner | Correlation; account collection |
| 2. Same display names | Schema designation and actual comparison | drao versus worker71001 is not friendly-name equality; check real fields and paths | Default always matches any visible full name | Correlation defaults |
| 3. Default path matches | Complete applicable logic | Configured no-match need not mean uncorrelated because documented default can match; validate owner | Only preferred configured pairing matters | Preset/default logic |
| 4. Wrong employee reference | Existing association versus correctness | Owner evidence contradicts matched value; correct actual input/relationship and assess downstream effects | Correlated flag or 100 percent proves legitimacy | Correlation; account management |
| 5. Missing intended identity | Population establishment versus app ownership | Investigate authoritative intake; do not make the app authoritative merely to reduce count | Every imported app account should create an employee identity | Identity profiles |
| 6. Criteria changed, data unchanged | Optimization and reevaluation | Verify documented unoptimized reexamination and affected records; read success isn't all-record evaluation | Optimized collection reruns every comparison | Correlation configuration; aggregation |
| 7. Manual link persists | Association history | Explicit manual link remains despite field change/unoptimized read; approved evidence-based correction required | Every current attribute change overrides manual link | Manual correlation |
| 8. Uncorrelated but enabled | Relationship versus target state | No contradiction; no access-removal operation supplied; verify legitimate use and approved action | Failed match automatically disables/deletes account | Account statuses; provisioning boundary |
| Project transfer / teach-back | Apply cause-based ownership reasoning to scale | Split missing identities/fields/configuration/shared/unused accounts, examine default/manual paths, verify sample owners/IDs/candidates and downstream effects | Assigning everything to administrator is legitimate because it clears count | Correlation; account types; Source Owner; processing/sync |

## COR-002

### Revision, review and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 7.2, **Which account field is ISC comparing with which identity field?** Documentation checked October 22, 2026. Documentation-based author review only; no tenant execution, observed beginner validation or formal publication approval. Existing lessons, tracing aid revision 1.0 and presentation/navigation code remain unchanged.

Independent fictional example: Devi's approved employee reference is text 0072002, including its leading zeros. HarborHR Production is authoritative for Employees; h-72002 is already attached to teaching identity I-72002 (not an actual object ID). HarborHR personNumber is text 0072002 and payrollRef is PAY-884. Applied Employees mapping supplies identity identificationNumber from personNumber, producing text 0072002. Identity name is worker72002 and displayName is Devi Rao.

WorkTrack Production is non-authoritative for employee establishment. Its accountKey/Account ID is wt-391, loginName/Account Name drao.wt, workerRef text 0072002 and displayName Devi Rao. The saved pairing is workerRef Equals identificationNumber. Identification Number is supplied as already eligible for correlation; intended reference uniquely identifies Devi, no other configured criterion supplies a different relationship, no identity name drao.wt supplies a default match, and no manual association overrides the case. Successful supported evaluation and association with I-72002 are fictional supplied results, not observations from a real tenant. No sync write or access-changing request is supplied.

### Official references and claim mapping

| Source | Sections or claims checked | Lesson boundary |

| [Assigning Source Accounts to Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Account/identity field pairing, standard Equals, configured case-insensitive default and ignoreCase setting, eligible searchable identity attributes and all-identity manual processing before aggregation, presets/default Account Name to identity name, persistent manual association and unoptimized reexamination | Same-named fields are neither required nor sufficient. No ambiguous-match winner, confidence score or unsupported cleanup behavior is invented. |

The initially opened generic Update Source API was not used to infer correlation behavior. The admin correlation documentation explicitly names the configuration's ignoreCase option and links its supported correlation API; this lesson does not provide an API payload, version-migration procedure or executable tenant-changing request. Identity-attribute API leads were checked for availability; searchable eligibility and preparation claims are anchored in the admin guide. Current official pages use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places; the course retains the requested ISC title without asserting every feature is licensed in every tenant.

### Example and reasoning checks

- JSON is a compact teaching grouping, not an API request or complete response. String 0072002 is preserved; integer conversion would produce 72002. Neither account key wt-391 nor payrollRef PAY-884 is the selected approved person reference.
- The first diagram shows HR-account-to-identity value provenance and a separate WorkTrack account-side input, then a configured comparison and supplied intended association. It does not draw a direct account-to-HR correlation join or undocumented private pipeline.
- WorkTrack accountKey selection is a left-side defect. Identity mapping to payrollRef is a right-side origin/meaning defect. A schema label, account inventory value, current identity value and saved criterion are separate evidence.
- Identity attributes searchable for correlation are distinct from Search filters. The guide documents a limited number of extra searchable attributes; no unnecessary capacity claim is put into this introductory field-selection explanation. Availability, mapping, populated value and required all-identity manual processing are not conflated.
- Case-insensitive configured behavior and per-configuration ignoreCase support do not establish whitespace or number normalization. Legacy abc7/ABC7 variation deliberately stops at ambiguous/unfit policy rather than inventing ISC tie-breaking.
- The circular workTrackWorkerRef variation is a dependency diagnosis under explicit unavailable-input assumptions, not a claim that every account-derived mapping fails or that all first accounts require manual correlation.
- Identity name worker72002 differs from designated Account Name drao.wt; friendly labels agree but do not establish the default path. Existing manual/ISC-created-account treatment is retained.
- Existing mapping Preview does not prove applied state, source reevaluation or association history. No automatic disable, permission grant or target result follows from the correlation statement.
- Contractor transfer distinguishes sponsor workerId from contractor externalPersonRef and independently mapped contractorRef; eligibility and preparation remain unresolved, so no completed association is claimed.

Prior lessons, tracing aid revision 1.0 and presentation/navigation code are unchanged.

Independent fictional example: Devi employee 73003, AnchorHR Production authoritative Employees, worker account hr-73003 already attached to teaching identity I-73003 (not an actual object ID). Employees maps AnchorHR personRef to identity identificationNumber; applied value is text P-73003. Identity name worker73003 and email drao@learn.example. TeamPlan Production is non-authoritative for employee establishment. Its tp-206 account key is Account ID, drao.tp login is Account Name, ownerPersonRef is P-73003 and mail is drao@learn.example. Owner verifies Devi. The reference is supplied under a fictional approved organizational policy as independently available, persistent across employment changes and not reassigned; those are not universal HR or ISC guarantees.

Identification Number is supplied as already eligible, intended identity reference is unique, no manual override is supplied, and no identity has the name drao.tp. Supported evaluation supplies the intended association tp-206 to I-73003. No sync write or access-changing request is configured as part of that result. A second-account variation uses tp-admin-206 with the same legitimate owner reference. Email-reassignment variation supplies stale old account mail and a different current holder, without claiming automatic movement of existing associations.

### Official references and claim mapping

| Source | Sections or claims checked | Lesson boundary |
|---|---|---|
| [Assigning Source Accounts to Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Paired account/identity values, fixed Equals, uniqueness guidance, ordered alternatives/one satisfied criterion, case/ignoreCase, sample Test, licensed recommendations, searchable identity eligibility and processing, preset/default paths and persistent manual associations | Identifier selection is an engineering decision based on documented comparison behavior plus supplied organizational guarantees. Separate rows do not declare a composite AND match. No duplicate-candidate winner, confidence score, automatic stale-link move or percentage-to-ownership guarantee is invented. |
| [Managing Source Account Schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Represented field definitions, Account ID/Name designations and post-aggregation change hazards | Distinguish source-account key and naming field from cross-system person reference. Do not change valid account keys or schema designations to imitate a better person identifier. |
| [Creating Identity Profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative establishment, mappings from different sources, profile-specific values and shared custom definitions, transforms, Preview and Apply Changes | Applied identity reference has its own origin; an approved persistent business reference does not guarantee retained ISC object continuity, automatic rehire recovery or restored access. Preview is not applied-state proof. |
| [Loading Account Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Collection and optimization; unoptimized reevaluation of unchanged accounts | Data availability on both sides must be established, and new configuration does not prove every account reevaluated. No broad rerun, destructive reset or universal timing recipe. |
| [Processing Identity Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Identity mapping evaluation, processing scope and manual preparation, requirements versus successful provisioning | Raw HR, current identity and collected app values are separate evidence. Better matching design does not prove delivered access or safe recovery. |
| [Managing Accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Human, machine and uncorrelated account classifications, represented account view, wrong associations and supported correction contexts | Shared/service responsibility is not automatically personal ownership. No account reset, deletion or manual reassignment procedure is executed or taught as count cleanup. |
| [Account Attribute transform](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/extensibility/transforms/operations/account-attribute/) | Lookups of accounts already on an identity and support for multiple associated accounts | First-account dependency assumes only missing application relationship supplies input. Repeat reads do not create independent ownership evidence; legitimate multiple accounts are not duplicate-person proof. |
| [Synchronizing Attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html) | Configured identity-to-existing-correlated-account writes and multi-account scope | Association/identity changes need consumer review. A guessed value copied into an account is not independent proof of owner; main example supplies no sync or access-changing work. |

Current official pages use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places. The course keeps the requested ISC title without implying every feature is available or licensed in every tenant. No API operation or payload is needed to understand identifier selection.

### Engineering reasoning, assumptions and limits

- Uniqueness, availability/coverage, consistency and stability are design evaluation dimensions, not four product status flags or guaranteed ISC identifier validation stages.
- Candidate scope and business identity matter. Local company uniqueness is not proof for a shared matching population. A profile label alone is not a documented restriction on the source's candidate identities; inspect the actual supported configuration.
- Account keys tp-206 and tp-admin-206 distinguish accounts. Shared P-73003 can legitimately identify one owner across them. Duplicate reference values on different identity records require investigation; the lesson does not predict ambiguous-candidate handling.
- Persistent personRef, employee number and employment/contract/payroll identifiers have distinct semantics. No blanket rule that employee number is always stable or email is never acceptable is asserted.
- Availability is temporal and dependency-based: independently linked HR can supply the identity reference before first app association. A proposed lookup needing that app relationship cannot bootstrap itself under the stated no-other-input assumptions.
- Qualified NORTH:73003 and SOUTH:73003 represent different fictional people. Removing the namespace creates collision. Both sides need the same approved qualification; no executable transform or composite-correlation implementation is supplied.
- Default case-insensitive behavior and configured ignoreCase support are documented. No implicit prefix stripping, whitespace cleanup, numeric conversion or type repair is assumed.
- Email reassignment is an explicit fictional business policy, not a SailPoint feature claim. Current uniqueness of the reused address does not prove correct owner of an old account. Existing manual associations are not assumed to move after value changes.
- The 1,000-account comparison supplies 1,000 email equalities and one confirmed wrong owner, versus 970 independently verified reference values and 30 missing. These give 100 percent value-match coverage and 97 percent reference availability. They are not interchangeable metrics, no 30-account overall uncorrelated result is asserted, and the other 999 email relationships are not declared verified.
- Rehire example explicitly supplies a deleted prior identity and unreviewed old access. Stable person meaning is not automatic object recovery, account recreation or authorization.
- The data contract and illustrative producer/application/integration/ISC responsibilities are an operating model to agree, not official mandatory job titles. No new sensitive personal data is collected or encouraged merely for matching.
- Main supplied match selects ownerPersonRef Equals identificationNumber. Default Account Name drao.tp has no identity-name match. No sync, permission grant, target field change or real tenant execution follows from the teaching result.

### Diagram and publication checks

- [Account keys and person references](../assets/COR-003_account_keys_and_person_references.txt), maximum 48 characters per line: two distinct accounts to one intended identity, separate duplicate-identity concern and human ownership check.
- [Reused address and stable reference](../assets/COR-003_reused_address_and_stable_reference.txt), maximum 49 characters per line: supplied earlier/later email ownership facts and separate persistent-reference path without promising automatic reassignment.
- Editable assets match inline blocks. Prose identifies arrows, conditions and excluded outcomes. The first is a relationship map, the second a temporal comparison, not private backend execution.
- Two two-column tables, eight worked cases and acquisition transfer stay in the learner file. Full published article, diagrams/tables, all forty-nine routes, seven-module native defaults/current-page links and unchanged tracing aid are publication checks; visual rendering is checked separately.
- No JSON/API payload is needed in this lesson. COR-004 remains planned. References and assessment metadata stay author-only.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Unique account key | Object and population meaning | Source record key has no supplied approved identity counterpart; uniqueness alone doesn't establish person | Any unique field is a person key | Correlation; schemas |
| 2. Two accounts, one owner | Many-account relationship | Distinct keys with one approved reference can identify one person; verify both uses/access | Repeated owner reference always duplicates people | Account Attribute; accounts |
| 3. Company-local numbers | Scope and qualification | Shared population has overlap; global/qualified reference must preserve meaning; two rows are not implicit AND | Local uniqueness or extra company row solves collision automatically | Correlation criteria; profile mappings |
| 4. Reused email | Stability and historical ownership | Current unique equality can identify new holder, not legitimate old account owner; inspect history | Unique current email proves continuity | Correlation; supplied reuse policy |
| 5. First-account input | Availability and dependency | No independent value under stated facts; provide approved input/exception, not another identical read | Field definition or repeat aggregation supplies unavailable data | Account Attribute lookup; profiles |
| 6. 100 versus 97 percent | Coverage versus correctness | Known wrong owner is defect; dependable reference plus remediation is candidate, not blanket go-live approval | Highest percentage certifies safest design | Sample Test/recommendations; engineering evidence |
| 7. Prefix collision | Meaning-preserving normalization | Preserve namespace; both producing/mapped values must retain distinction | Easier equality always improves matching | Correlation comparisons; transforms/mappings |
| 8. Returning employee | Reference versus object/access continuity | Deleted identity and old access need approved identity/account/access handling independently | Stable business reference restores old ISC object/access | Profiles; accounts; processing |
| Acquisition transfer / teach-back | Apply full decision framework | Resolve overlapping scopes, missing contractor coverage and downstream-email dependency; verify qualified/global supplier, eligibility, criteria/default/history, actual owner outcomes and deliberate non-matches | Temporary email fallback and removing prefixes bypass design gaps | Correlation; mappings; input dependencies |

Learner validation and formal approval remain pending.

## COR-004

### Revision, review and limits

Revision 1.0, Module 7.4, **The first comparison found no match. What happens next?** Documentation checked October 22, 2026. Documentation-based author review only; no tenant execution, observed beginner validation or formal publication approval. All prior lessons, tracing aid revision 1.0 and presentation/navigation code are unchanged.

Independent fictional example: Devi 74004; PeopleLedger Production authoritative Employees; pl-74004 already associated with teaching identity I-74004 (not an actual object ID). Applied identificationNumber is text 74004, email devi.rao@learn.example, identity name worker74004. Other identity I-74005 has identificationNumber 74005, email other.worker@learn.example and name worker74005. Candidate evidence excludes any identity with identificationNumber OLD-74004 or name drao.cp; email comparison uniquely matches Devi.

CasePort Production is non-authoritative for employee establishment. cp-419 account key is Account ID; loginName drao.cp is Account Name; employeeRef OLD-74004; mail DEVI.RAO@learn.example; friendly display Devi Rao. Complete saved configured list is employeeRef Equals identificationNumber, then mail Equals email. Both identity attributes are eligible and applied, configured case-insensitive comparison is supplied, account is evaluated and no existing manual override applies. First configured criterion supplies no match, second supplies the intended association cp-419 to I-74004. The successful link does not repair the old employee-reference input; its origin still needs investigation. No sync write or access-changing request is supplied.

Independent cp-420 variation has neither configured match, designated Account Name WORKER74004, unique intended identity name worker74004 and approved Devi ownership, with supplied default association. cp-421 has neither configured match nor a default name match for unmatched421, no manual/other supplied match and an uncorrelated result. These are teaching facts, not tenant observations or a real schema/naming change.

### Official references and claim mapping

| Source | Sections or claims checked | Lesson boundary |
|---|---|---|
| [Assigning Source Accounts to Identities](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Paired attributes, fixed Equals, ordered one-pair-at-a-time criteria with a criterion able to establish association, case/ignoreCase, sample Test, searchable eligibility/preparation, presets, default Account Name to identity name, manual persistence and ISC-created-account treatment | Main trace uses first no-match then one unique intended second match. Standard rows do not imply AND, weighted voting or later mandatory confirmation. Default applies if configured correlation cannot associate; no unverified winner/continuation algorithm for ambiguous or error conditions is asserted. |
| [Managing Source Account Schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Fields and Account ID/Name designations, post-aggregation change risks | Default checks designated loginName, not friendly displayName. cp-420 is a different account value, not a changed schema designation. No rename/key reset procedure is supplied. |
| [Creating Identity Profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_profiles.html) | Authoritative establishment, profile identity attribute mappings, custom fields, Preview and Apply Changes | Candidate values are supplied as applied, eligible current values. A proposed mapping preview is not applied identity state or an account-correlation execution. |
| [Loading Account Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) | Collection, optimized unchanged-account skipping, unoptimized reevaluation, activity/history scope/status/optimization evidence | Successful collection differs from each criterion's evaluation. Configuration changes do not prove unchanged accounts reevaluated; no broad destructive rerun or universal timing recipe. |
| [Managing Accounts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/index.html) | Collected account view, attributes, uncorrelated state, wrong correlations and correction context | Missing relationship is distinct from external account removal/disable. Correct ownership needs approved evidence; no account deletion/reset/manual move is performed. |
| [Viewing Identity Control Panels](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/identities/control_panel.html) | Identity profile/current attributes, represented access and associated-account evidence, events | Record current source/account/identity and observation context. No nonexistent criterion-by-criterion UI log or complete historical reconstruction is invented. |
| [Processing Identity Data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/setup/identity_processing.html) | Applied mappings, manual processing and requirements-versus-provisioning boundary | Searchable preparation and identity value evidence are separate from source comparison/evaluation and successful target delivery. |
| [Synchronizing Attributes](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/attr_sync.html) | Configured identity-to-existing-correlated-account writes | A real identity/relationship correction can affect consumers; main supplied correlation has no sync/access write and does not prove target state. |

Only primary ISC admin documentation supports product claims. Search results about IdentityIQ rules, multi-host/resource-specific correlation and unrelated products are not used to define this standard account-correlation lesson. Current official pages use Identity Services / Human Fabric naming in places; the course keeps the requested ISC title without universal licensing claims.

### Reasoning boundaries and checks

- The distinction is criterion no-match versus configured no-association versus overall uncorrelated result, not a claim that the product exposes these as three official status flags.
- Main selected strings are OLD-74004 versus 74004/74005 and DEVI.RAO@learn.example versus devi.rao@learn.example. Case-insensitive equality supplies only the second unique intended match. No prefix stripping, whitespace cleanup or numeric conversion is invented.
- The standard form uses fixed Equals and ordered alternatives. AND requires conditions together; OR needs an alternative. There is no majority score or requirement that later rows confirm an already supported configured match.
- Order is actual saved configuration evidence, not an ownership guarantee. Conflicting rows and duplicate candidates are investigated; the lesson does not claim source collection order, identity recency, profile priority or hidden confidence selects the right person.
- Configured ignoreCase behavior is distinct from the non-configurable case-insensitive default naming path. A case-sensitive configured email example changes that criterion's result, not automatically the whole process.
- cp-420 and cp-421 explicitly supply all configured/default no-match or unique-match conditions. The current friendly name does not establish the default, and a configured match is not followed by a default replacement.
- Null/empty/unavailable input and an evaluation error are distinct evidence. The simple diagram does not imply every abnormal state falls through identically.
- Optimization and manual correlation are independent boundaries: optimized unchanged records can be skipped; unoptimized aggregation is not an automatic unlink/move command for persistent manual associations.
- Current reproducible comparison is not historical execution proof. Where actual per-criterion evidence isn't available, absence is stated rather than inventing a log or private pipeline.
- Contractor transfer has wrong-person sponsor reference, unavailable future contractor email and an independent reference not yet implemented on both sides. No safe completed association is inferred.
- Main example has no source/schema/criteria/account/identity/correlation/reset/deletion/sync/target operation. No tenant/API execution or learner result is claimed.

### Diagrams and publication checks

- [Configured comparisons and default](../assets/COR-004_configured_comparisons_and_default.txt), maximum 37 characters per line: first configured no-match, second unique intended match, default only if configured matching cannot establish relationship, and independent human verification.
- [Configuration and reevaluation](../assets/COR-004_configuration_and_reevaluation.txt), maximum 38 characters per line: saved configuration, unchanged-account skip, supported reevaluation and persistent-manual boundary; no guaranteed movement.
- Assets match inline blocks. Prose explains arrows, explicit conditions and omitted ambiguous/error behavior, rather than drawing private backend services.
- Two two-column tables, eight worked cases and contractor-source transfer remain in the article. Complete live text/diagrams/tables, all fifty routes, seven-module native defaults/current-page links and unchanged tracing aid are publication checks; visual rendering is checked separately.
- No JSON or executable API example is necessary here. COR-005 remains planned. Source citations and assessment metadata remain author-only.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. First criterion no-match | Criterion versus overall result | Unique intended second configured match can supply association; verify recorded result and owner | First no-match means uncorrelated overall | Ordered configured correlation |
| 2. One match, one no-match | Alternatives versus conjunction | Standard second row isn't an automatic veto or all-fields confirmation | Multiple rows mean AND | Configured criterion semantics |
| 3. Configured no-match, naming match | Separate default path | WORKER74004 matches worker74004 case-insensitively under supplied unique candidate/evaluation; friendly name irrelevant | Configured no-match eliminates default | Default correlation; schema designations |
| 4. Changed case setting | Actual comparison settings | Don't reuse default case-insensitive conclusion; inspect configured row then other paths separately | One familiar value comparison establishes every configured/default outcome | ignoreCase versus default |
| 5. Duplicate identity email | Insufficient candidate evidence | Investigate duplicates/owner and actual supported result; no first-display winner | Candidate display or collection order is known tie-breaker | Uniqueness guidance; accounts |
| 6. Contradictory fields | Order versus ownership correctness | Preserve order/result and repair proven input cause; preferred ordering doesn't repair wrong data | Ordering a preferred identifier legitimizes conflicting values | Ordered alternatives; wrong correlation context |
| 7. Manual link persists | Association history | Persistent explicit association isn't cleared by unoptimized reread; approved correction requires evidence | Every current field match overrides existing manual link | Manual/ISC-created-account treatment |
| 8. New criteria, unchanged input | Actual evaluation | Optimized read may skip unchanged record; establish supported reevaluation and record result | Read success proves all new criteria ran | Aggregation optimization |
| Contractor-source transfer / teach-back | Full configured/default/input/result trace | Sponsor comparison can point to wrong person, future email unavailable, rows aren't AND; establish independent reference/mapping/eligibility and default/manual/evaluation evidence then actual owner outcome | More rows or later email promise safe contractor ownership | Correlation; profiles; optimization; accounts |

Learner validation and formal approval remain pending.

## ARC-005

Supporting IQService, identity/account/access-profile and source-correlation meanings retain earlier checks on the same date.

- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 35005, OrderDesk, orders-35005, svc-order-isc, orders.export, Orders-Prod, source/cluster/VA names, audit and network incidents, and ResearchArchive are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: operational dependency reasoning and evidence across actual runtime paths, not a full network/protocol course, connector setup runbook, universal API error dictionary, or private platform execution algorithm.

### Official sources by claim and section

|---|---|
| [VA system and network requirements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/requirements_va.html) | Relevant runtime's DNS, time synchronization, required cloud and target connectivity, target-specific ports and private-CA/inspection trust conditions. The lesson doesn't freeze changeable endpoint/IP/port lists or treat cloud connectivity as a target test. |
| [VA configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/config_va.html) | HTTP proxy route includes applicable source HTTP/HTTPS traffic; actual configuration and supported exceptions matter. Proxy admission differs from target admission. No proxy credentials, bootstrap commands, bypass changes or unrestricted firewall opening are prescribed. |
| [VA deployment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/deploy_va.html) and [management](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/manage_va.html) | Source-to-cluster/member execution and health scope retained from ARC-002. Main member selection is supplied evidence, not a fixed worker algorithm. A different VA or laptop check isn't substituted for the actual member. |
