This short example isn't a timeless feature table or a recommendation to change Salesforce settings. |
| [Account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Represented fields, designated account identifier and input interpretation. A display label isn't automatically an operation key. No schema designation changes or runnable operation payloads are proposed. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) and [provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) | Source/recipient/action/progress/result/error context and supported access-changing work versus represented reads. The lesson distinguishes repaired dependencies, recovered/reconciled work and verified target outcomes; it doesn't promise immediate refresh, rollback or universal retries. |
| [HTTP semantics, RFC 9110](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html), sections 3.7, 9.2.2, 15.5.1, 15.5.2, 15.5.4 and 15.5.8 | Supporting intermediary, idempotence and 400/401/403/407 meanings. These classify responses, not unique ISC root causes. Fictional error details and known target effects supply the case conclusions; protocol definitions aren't connector recovery instructions. |
| [Additional HTTP codes, RFC 6585](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6585.html), section 4 | Supporting 429 rate-limit meaning only. No universal retry delay, counter, immediate-repeat guarantee or claim that every target uses this code. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) and [access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Supporting ownership association and single-source access bundle retain earlier documentation checks. Main correlation, approval, account choice and automatic provisioning eligibility are explicit example assumptions, not an account-selection or lifecycle algorithm. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi, employee 35005, already has correctly correlated orders-35005. She can view summaries but requires the distinct orders.export entitlement. Export order summaries contains only that entitlement on OrderDesk Production in Orders-Prod.
- The main hypothetical connector supports automatic assignment to the existing account. No real OrderDesk connector is claimed. No account creation, custom script or IQService leg is required in this example.
- C-Order-Prod has va-order-01/va-order-02. Supplied evidence identifies member 02 and a direct production API route for the main attempt. An alternate proxy case is a different deployment, not a hidden route change in the main incident.
- svc-order-isc is the external integration actor. It is distinct from the recipient, an ISC administrator and a caller of ISC's own API. No token value, password, secret key, real endpoint or executable command is supplied.
- Destination, resolution/reachability, protected service identity, accepted caller, resource-specific authority, supported connector/configuration and valid target inputs are related prerequisites. The table isn't a universal sequence; connector rejection can occur before target contact.
- The main audit explicitly identifies the caller, workspace, account and missing permission-assignment privilege, and confirms absent export access. The correct narrow conclusion relies on those supplied facts, not green connection/read status.
- The DNS case supplies member-specific failure before any target session; the proxy case supplies origin and no forwarding; the invalid-input case supplies precise 400 validation detail and no applied change. Those facts aren't inferred from generic error codes or absence of logs.
- HTTP status numbers don't universally identify the responder, accepted credential, missing role, account or completed effect. IQService and directory messages aren't reduced to HTTP codes.
- The two-leg IQService example preserves the distinction between corrected trust, registered-client admission and target authority. Required security remains enabled; no credential reset is inferred from unrelated reachability evidence.
- The timeout case explicitly supplies target audit/current state establishing applied access. No automatic rollback, identical retry response or universal idempotence follows. Current collected ISC data, reported operation state and actual target state remain separate.
- Rate limiting and repeated-work behavior follow the selected connector/target documentation. The lesson doesn't issue unsupported blanket retries, timing estimates or schedule changes.
- ResearchArchive is a separate fictional design case. A read-only SaaS source's established reads don't implement target disable; appropriate supported capability/configuration, destination, authority and outcome evidence must be obtained before approving the design.
- Ownership and handoffs depend on the actual organization. Relevant error context is shared without secrets or unrestricted sensitive trace files. No real incident response, network change or API invocation occurred.
- Current official naming includes Identity Services / Human Fabric. ISC remains the requested course name. Feature visibility/downloads aren't license promises.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Runtime paths](../assets/ARC-005_runtime_paths.txt): selected VA API, hosted SaaS connector and applicable IQService work relationships; not all possible protocols or packet routes.
- [Evidence and recovery](../assets/ARC-005_evidence_and_recovery.txt): human investigation decisions; insufficient evidence returns to assessment, while a justified scoped action leads to recovery/reconciliation and target verification.
- Assets match inline text exactly. Maximum line widths are 44 and 50 characters. Captions explain returning results, outbound VA initiation, omitted implementation detail and the distinction between investigation and platform execution.
- Local/live checks separately verify complete prose, tables, diagrams, headings, placement and navigation. These aren't beginner comprehension or tenant-execution tests.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Main denied-export trace | Narrow diagnosis from operation/caller/resource facts | Supplied production audit establishes missing integration privilege; validate scoped correction and recover/verify change | Green test/read proves write privilege; recipient password or DNS explains explicit target denial | Authorization examples; monitoring; supplied audit |
| Member-specific resolution | Actual runtime and pre-contact defect | Inspect member 02's configured hostname/resolver path; retain security; recover and verify | Laptop/other-member resolution proves all members; target password repairs DNS | VA network/deployment requirements |
| Corrected TLS, unregistered client | More than one prerequisite can fail | TLS passed for new attempt; repair approved IQService client admission, not unrelated target authority | New downstream refusal means first correction was wrong | IQService TLS/client authentication and role distinctions |
| Missing workspace input | Valid authority versus invalid request data | Supplied 400 detail identifies mapping/value requirement; validate correct input | Every client error is credential rejection; privileges supply missing fields | Schema/mapping meanings; HTTP semantics; supplied validation facts |
| Proxy 407 | Error origin and distinct admission contexts | Examine supported proxy authentication/path; no target-token reset inferred | Every authentication-looking error belongs to the target | VA proxy configuration; HTTP 407 semantics |
| Applied change, lost response | Actual effect versus recorded timeout/older data | Supplied target evidence establishes assignment; reconcile tracked work and collected view without blind repeat | Timeout/older represented view proves no effect or automatic rollback | Monitoring; idempotence semantics; supplied target evidence |
| Read-only project transfer | Capability-first design reasoning | Existing reads are established; obtain supported exact-operation/account-type path and authority/input/outcome validation | Administrator credentials add missing connector functionality; one green test approves a new write design | Source read-only definitions; capability and authorization examples |

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Introduction to IQService](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/intro.html) | Native Windows service, supported Windows API work, supported server/target connectivity, one instance serving multiple sources and shared resources. IQService isn't ISC hosted on Windows or a replacement for a VA. The current version string isn't copied into a timeless lesson. Separate IQService instances are required if both ISC and IdentityIQ are deployed; no IdentityIQ deployment procedure is imported here. |
| [IQService supported sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/sources_supporting.html) | Operation-specific dependency includes provisioning, selected attribute reads, and reads/test operations for applicable source types. The learner isn't given an entire low-use connector catalog. Read versus write alone doesn't decide IQService participation. |
| [ISC AD IQService settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/iqservice_settings.html) | IQService required for all AD provisioning and particular fields such as Terminal Services/Lync; configured host/port/client credentials and TLS; auto-update requires UpdateService. The lesson explains the remote-session example briefly and doesn't teach obsolete communications tooling. Optional-looking form labels don't override current security prerequisites. |
| [ISC AD IQService prerequisite](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/iqservice_as_a_prerequisite.html) | Supported Windows-side service and operation requirements; TLS enforced by current installation/default and mandatory security requirement; automatic AD revocation/password operations are also applicable, without expanding this lesson into those primary topics. No TLS downgrade is suggested. |
| [ISC AD prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/prerequisites.html), [forest settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/forest_settings.html), and [supported features](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/active_directory/help/integrating_active_directory/supported_features.html) | VA cluster requirement, target service account and operation support, separate directory connection settings and operation/account-type conditions. The supplied basic read uses a path/field selection not requiring the failed IQService interaction. No claim that every aggregation bypasses IQService: specific attributes, features and service-account choices can add dependencies. No complete Kerberos, gMSA, forest trust or global-catalog recipe is taught. |
| [IQService prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/system_requirements.html) | Distinct source service-account context, registered IQService client user, Windows service logon and applicable PowerShell script context. Roles are distinguished, not required to have different usernames universally. Windows/.NET/resource lists are deferred to current requirements rather than frozen in theory prose. |
| [Secure VA/IQService communication](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/secure_communication.html) and [IQService security](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/security.html) | TLS plus client authentication; registered user credentials checked through Windows before request processing. Service admission is distinct from target authorization. The warning about unprotected command execution supports retaining security controls, not disabling them for diagnosis. |
| [TLS and client-authentication configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/common/topics/configuring_tls_and_client_authentication_for_iqservice.html) | Server certificate identity, associated private key, trust and required host/VA configuration; registered client and source credentials; separate IQService-to-target protection. Conceptual trust/admission explanation only, no key export/import commands, fixed port, certificate-validation bypass or unrestricted inbound exposure. |
| [Required IQService permissions](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/min_permission_config.html) | Local service resources, logging/files and certificate/private-key access; supported minimum-permission arrangements are source/feature-specific. The lesson doesn't generalize a normal-user configuration to every feature or recommend blanket Domain Admin privileges. |
| [Before/after scripts](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/before_after_scripts.html) | Native connector-side scripts around processing, applicable request/result modification, contextual permissions and ScriptExecutor/TLS prerequisites. No script is required in the main group-assignment case; native scripts aren't conflated with every hosted/cloud rule type. |
| [Enable/disable IQService services](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/option_to_disable.html) | Operation handlers versus Windows process health; current ScriptExecutor disabled default and TLS plus explicit enablement when justified. Sensitive unrelated handlers and command lists aren't reproduced or indiscriminately enabled. |
| [IQService troubleshooting](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/troubleshooting.html) | Network, TLS, client/handler and script dependencies; after-provisioning rule executes regardless of provisioning outcome. The main certificate rejection and separate account-denial facts are supplied examples, not an actual diagnostic run. Legacy non-TLS workarounds appearing elsewhere on the page aren't copied over current TLS requirements. |
| [Updating IQService](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/iqservice/help/integrating_iqservice_admin/iqservice_update.html) | Configured UpdateService, documented VA-delivered update packages, host/credential/trust readiness, backup and maintenance impact. Applicable deployment support is qualified: the documentation excludes automatic updating for certain 32-bit deployments, so the lesson doesn't promise universal automatic updates. No update commands or version-specific installation procedure are copied. |
| [VA overview](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/index.html), [VA deployment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/deploy_va.html), and [VA management](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/manage_va.html) | Supporting VA/cluster, source association, outbound cloud initiation and Connected-state scope verified in ARC-002. Two VAs don't create an alternative IQService endpoint. No exact worker choice, zero-interruption guarantee or unsupported Windows-service topology is inferred. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html), [correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html), and [access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html) | Supporting tracked operation versus target observation, correct person/account association, and one-source bundle. Approval and account selection are supplied assumptions; target membership and represented collected state remain distinct. |

### Conditions and limits

- Devi, employee 34004, is an engineering coordinator with an existing correctly correlated eng-34004 account. No new account creation, multi-account selection algorithm, custom native script or additional access dependency is assumed in the main path.
- Read engineering documents bundles only ENG-Docs-Readers from Engineering Directory. Document-reading behavior is fictional and remains subject to the document system's own checks; membership isn't claimed to terminate/create sessions or prove every business action.
- The source uses the ISC Active Directory connector and C-Eng-Prod with va-eng-01/va-eng-02. Both share the configured Windows IQService endpoint iq-win-01.corp.example. Names aren't deployment commands, real credentials or a fixed IP/port design.
- CORP\svc-ad-isc supplies the target-operation context; CORP\svc-iq-client is the registered client user; CORP\svc-iq-run runs the Windows service. eng-34004 is the recipient. Shared-account arrangements can combine names without combining the conceptual checks.
- Source configuration, VA connectivity, IQService listener availability, TLS server identity/trust, client registration/Windows authentication, handler availability, target authority and actual outcome are separate questions.
- The main incident supplies a certificate-expiry rejection before admission through that connection and confirms absent target membership. That isn't inferred for every failure or timeout. A successful basic read isn't generalized to every field, connector or account-authentication option.
- The successful read's route is explained in prose; the operation diagram covers the selected group-provisioning route. It isn't an assertion that every AD read or every network packet follows the IQService chain.
- TLS/client authentication are required for the stated arrangement. Client registration doesn't create the Windows account or grant directory privileges; valid server trust doesn't establish successful client authentication or target authorization.
- Native before/after customization has separate execution context, capability and outcome. After-script execution isn't a primary-success signal; universal transaction rollback or script-result semantics aren't promised.
- ScriptExecutor's current disabled default is a checked current fact, not a timeless installation command. Enabling unneeded handlers, disabling TLS, bypassing certificate checks, copying private keys into support conversations or opening unrestricted firewall access isn't proposed.
- IQService/UpdateService automatic updating requires supported configuration. The Windows host remains a customer-managed component. Shared service maintenance affects operations using that component; VA redundancy doesn't resolve it.
- The lesson doesn't prescribe restarting/reinstalling as a first diagnostic action, implement a load balancer, guarantee failover, teach a complete gMSA or Windows-privilege model, or claim an IQService runtime test.
- Current official branding varies between ISC and Identity Services / Human Fabric. The requested course uses ISC consistently and doesn't treat download availability as a license grant.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Operation path](../assets/ARC-004_operation_path.txt): ISC decision/prepared work, VA connector work, IQService Windows-side work and target operation, scoped to the supplied group assignment.
- [Security boundaries](../assets/ARC-004_security_boundaries.txt): reachability, server trust/protected channel, admitted client, required capability, target authority and subsequent outcome inspection.
- Assets match the inline blocks exactly. Maximum line widths are 49 and 44 characters. Prose explains returned results, VA outbound initiation, conditional success, omitted internals and the human verification in the final security-diagram box.
- Local and live checks separately verify full teaching text, headings, tables, diagrams, placement and navigation. These aren't beginner comprehension or tenant-execution tests.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Basic read succeeds | Operation/field-specific dependencies | Inspect required provisioning path; basic read and visible collected account don't validate IQService | Successful account collection proves every connector operation works or IQService isn't needed | AD IQService settings/prerequisites |
| Expired certificate | Secured connection failure versus target authority | Repair approved identity/trust configuration retaining TLS/client authentication; recover and verify operation separately | Disabling security or expanding AD privilege repairs TLS identity/validity | TLS/client authentication; AD security requirement; supplied incident |
| Unregistered client | IQService registration versus Windows existence | Register correct approved client and source credential; no target privilege expansion inferred | Existing Windows account is automatically registered with IQService | Secure communication; TLS/client registration |
| Target group change denied | Distinct request-admission/process/target credentials | Supplied target audit identifies source target-management user; validate scoped permission and intended object | Service logon or client user is universally the target actor | Prerequisites service-account contexts; supplied audit facts |
| After script message | Primary operation outcome versus customization activity | Reconcile primary result, script effects and actual target membership separately | After means only after success; success-looking script output proves provisioning | Native scripts; troubleshooting after-provisioning result |
| Two VAs, one stopped service | Shared dependencies and maintenance | Both paths still require same IQService; identify affected sources/work and supported recovery/availability design | VA redundancy automatically supplies IQService redundancy | IQService introduction; VA source association; update/maintenance conditions |
| Different-connector read transfer | Independent operation requirement and capability evidence | Follow documented read dependency; Running isn't enabled handler or completed read; validate security, admission and actual read | IQService is universally write-only and process health is operation success | Supported sources; enabled/disabled handlers; troubleshooting |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Reference evidence, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## ARC-003

- Lesson: [ARC-003](../lessons/ARC-003_SaaS_Connectors_and_Their_Connection_Path.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 3.3.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026; source definitions, SaaS runtime overview, migration, example connector authentication/capabilities, account loading/schema, provisioning/monitoring, and supporting OAuth definitions reviewed. Access-profile and correlation meanings also retain the documented checks recorded in earlier lessons on the same date.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. ProjectBoard is a fictional application and connector, not a verified connector offering. Employee 33003, accounts, integration client, workspaces, access value, target behavior and incident evidence are invented.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: conceptual and operational SaaS connector path, external API authority, read/change distinctions, evidence and supported migration boundaries. No custom connector coding, OAuth implementation recipe, private backend topology, full migration procedure, product entitlement promise, or universal retry/refresh behavior is taught.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Source/connector distinctions; direct connections can use VA-based or SaaS connectors; SaaS needs no on-premise VA cluster; read-only SaaS configurations exist as well as deeper governance options. Execution location and operation capability are different questions. No claim that all SaaS connectors provision or that every application has both paths. |
| [SaaS connectivity overview](https://developer.sailpoint.com/docs/connectivity/saas-connectivity/) | Hosted connector runtime rather than customer VA execution; cloud runtime cannot simply reach intranet-only services; VA and SaaS paths can coexist. The diagrams are logical boundaries, not copied private infrastructure architecture. The source's credential-encryption discussion isn't generalized to unsupported secret handling. |
| [Virtual Appliances](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/index.html) and [deployment](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/deploy_va.html) | Supporting VA/cluster meanings, outbound-initiated cloud communication, source/cluster association, and shared cluster dependencies. Prior ARC-002 source verification is retained; ARC-003 doesn't reproduce an installation recipe or infer that every external interface is an API. |
| [Migrating VA-based sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/migrate_sources.html) | Listed eligible sources only; prepare draft while live source operates; reenter encrypted values; permanent non-revertible apply; schema compatibility/dependencies; possible scheduling effects from time-zone differences. Source migration isn't permission to delete a cluster still used elsewhere. The source was reviewed, not an actual migration run. |
| [Box SaaS connection settings](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/box/help/saas_connectivity/box/connection_settings.html) | Concrete primary-source evidence that SaaS connection configuration can include external enterprise ID, client ID/secret and authentication-specific key values. These fields aren't a universal connector schema; the lesson doesn't claim ProjectBoard uses Box authentication or copy key-generation steps. |
| [Google Workspace SaaS prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/googleworkspace/help/saas_connectivity/google_workspace/prerequisites.html) | External authorization context, supported OAuth methods, consent and user/impersonation roles can affect API authority. Token scope alone isn't a complete universal permission model. Google-specific roles, licensing and extra product setup aren't presented as ISC-wide prerequisites. |
| [Salesforce SaaS supported features](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/salesforce/help/saas_connectivity/salesforce/supported_features.html) | Operation/account-type-specific capability and activation conditions. Current documentation supports account operations but excludes enable/disable for portal/partner users. The learner text uses this only as evidence that feature exceptions exist, not a Salesforce configuration lesson or timeless feature table. |
| [Salesforce VA-based prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/salesforce/help/integrating_salesforce/prerequisites.html) | Concrete counterexample to cloud application implies no VA: this documented connector path requires a VA cluster. The SaaS path is a separate option. Authentication recommendations change, so no legacy Basic/OAuth setup recommendation is copied into the lesson. |
| [SuccessFactors SaaS prerequisites](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/connectors/saas/successfactors/help/saas_connectivity/successfactors/prerequisites.html) | External API privileges and network-origin restrictions can still matter with SaaS connectivity. Diagnostic reasoning doesn't authorize removing all restrictions or invent a fixed IP allowlist. |
| [Loading account data](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/loading_data.html) and [account schemas](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/schema.html) | Aggregation collects account information; supported scheduled/on-demand entry points, collection activity and scope; schema fields and designated account identifiers. A first API page isn't a completed collection. No deletion behavior or correlation reevaluation is inferred from a partial failed example. |
| [Account correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html), [access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html), and [request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html) | Supporting person/account association, one-source entitlement bundle and configured request review. Correct correlation, recipient/account choice and available provisioning are supplied assumptions; no full account-selection or eligibility algorithm is invented. |
| [Provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/index.html) and [monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Changing access versus reading it; account/source/recipient/action/result/error evidence; tracked operation versus target state. Applicable automatic provisioning is assumed explicitly. No guaranteed immediate refresh, universal retry, rollback, session termination or timeout-equals-no-effect claim. |
| [OAuth 2.0 framework, RFC 6749](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749.html), sections 1.4, 1.5, 2.2, 2.3 and 3.3 | Supporting access-token, refresh-token, client identifier/authentication and scope meanings only. This foundational definitions source isn't a recommendation to implement every historical OAuth flow. Current supported connector authentication requirements govern actual configuration. |

### Conditions and limits

- ProjectBoard Production is the ISC source label. Delivery-Prod is the intended external workspace; Delivery-Test is a separate external workspace. A duplicate account string in test doesn't establish production identity or correct destination.
- Devi's existing project-33003 account is already correctly associated with her ISC identity. Employee number 33003 isn't silently treated as a source account key.
- Integration client isc-project-prod uses the configured fictional API token. It is distinct from the recipient, an ISC administrator, and an API caller authenticated to ISC itself. No usable credential, endpoint, token payload or API command is supplied.
- The main successful change explicitly assumes a supported write-capable SaaS connector, required source provisioning configuration, approved request, correct destination/account, and no creation dependency. It isn't a promise that every approval delivers access.
- The View projects access profile contains only project.view on this source. ProjectBoard's fictional entitlement grants viewing, not editing. Addition/removal execution is separate from business justification.
- The read/write diagram isn't a guaranteed cycle. It doesn't require every write to cause aggregation, immediate UI refresh, or a particular internal queue.
- Browser reachability and a connection test have limited evidence scope. Source account pages represent collected information; activity records describe tracked work; target/audit observations establish the supplied external result at their observation time.
- OAuth terminology supplies supporting context, not ProjectBoard's authentication mechanism. Effective authority can depend on scope plus external roles/context; automatic token renewal isn't assumed.
- Pagination, 100-record first page, continuation marker, throttling, invalid token and timeout outcomes are explicitly supplied hypothetical API behavior. No product-wide page size, throttling threshold, retry interval, absent-account deletion behavior or exactly-once claim follows.
- Correcting a prerequisite doesn't complete previously failed work. Target state and supported recovery must be reconciled. Least privilege isn't a recommendation to grant unrestricted target administration.
- A private management API remains a connectivity constraint even when a human website is public. Network restrictions and endpoint reachability must be checked from the actual supported runtime path.
- VA-to-SaaS migration is not universal or reversible after apply. Configuration/schema/authentication/time-zone review and other sources' cluster associations remain relevant. The lesson isn't a runbook or authority to change live infrastructure.
- Current official pages use Identity Services / Human Fabric branding. ISC is retained as the requested course name. Feature visibility, activation and commercial packaging aren't promised by this theory lesson.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [SaaS and VA paths](../assets/ARC-003_saas_and_va_paths.txt): hosted runtime versus customer VA-based work, each interacting with the external API in an API-based example.
- [Read and change](../assets/ARC-003_read_and_change.txt): external data into ISC versus a supported approved change delivered to the external account.
- Assets match the inline blocks exactly. Maximum line widths are 39 and 51 characters. Nearby text explains responses, VA outbound initiation, conditional success, external enforcement, and omitted implementation detail.
- Local and live publication checks separately verify complete text, headings, tables, diagrams, placement and navigation. They aren't learner validation or observed tenant execution.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cloud-hosted application | Hosting versus connector execution mode | Inspect configured connector requirements; preserve VA until a supported alternative and change are validated | Every hosted business application uses SaaS connector execution | Sources; runtime overview; Salesforce VA prerequisites |
| Read works, change denied | Authentication/read success versus write authorization | Supplied production audit context identifies correct caller and explicit write denial; validate scoped privilege, recover and verify | Approval, read access, recipient password or ISC admin level supplies target write authority | Connector prerequisites; provisioning/monitoring |
| Wrong workspace | Destination and account context | Successful test write isn't a production grant; examine configuration and target context, review unintended change and intended outcome | Source display name or matching account string establishes production destination | Connector connection settings; supplied target facts |
| Rotated token | Credential lifecycle versus runtime placement | Update revoked token through supported procedure, protect secrets, verify affected work | Another VA repairs SaaS credential rejection; external rotation automatically updates source | External authentication settings; supplied invalid-token evidence |
| First page only | Collection completeness and failure reasoning | Continuation proves more records; investigate throttle/scope/recovery without assuming deletion | Missing from partial response proves removal or a completed aggregation | Account loading; supplied pagination/throttling facts |
| Green migration draft | Test scope, irreversible transition, shared dependencies | Validate required behavior/schema/timing before permanent apply; retain cluster for remaining source | Green test validates everything; migrating one source retires shared infrastructure | Migration; VA source associations |
| Private payroll API transfer | Independent path and evidence reasoning | Hosted runtime needs supported API reachability; public browser site isn't that path; validate exact operations and target outcomes separately | Cloud hosting supplies private route and one test proves every change | SaaS runtime constraints; connection/capability references |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Documentation, assessment intent and review limitations stay outside it.

## ARC-002

- Lesson: [ARC-002](../lessons/ARC-002_Virtual_Appliances_and_Clusters.md), revision 1.0.
- Course placement: Module 3.2.
- Documentation checked: October 20, 2026; core VA sources read for this lesson. Supporting object and provisioning definitions retain the documented checks recorded earlier on the same date.
- Author review: completed against teaching specification v2.6 for the stated scope; no unresolved author content issue identified.
- Tenant execution: not performed. Employee 32002, AssetDesk, account/entitlement/cluster/VA names, host and DNS failure, interrupted-response case, and Payroll transfer are fictional.
- Learner validation and formal publication approval: pending. Working-edition deployment is separate.
- Scope: core ISC VA-based source connectivity. No IdentityIQ-specific single-VA exception, complete installation recipe, real diagnostic run, private worker topology, guaranteed retry/deduplication semantics, or general-purpose hypervisor migration procedure is taught.

### Official sources by claim and section

| Official reference | Claim / section and applicability |
|---|---|
| [SailPoint Virtual Appliances](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/index.html) | Linux-based customer-deployed VM, hosted tenant versus integration role, vendor-managed software, customer hosting/connectivity/health, outbound-only cloud communication and polling. The lesson separates network initiation from logical work direction and response data. |
| [Deploying VAs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/deploy_va.html) | Each source's specific cluster, cluster-directed requests/queue, running-member polling, either-member work in documented running-member arrangements, pairing and saved-but-unpaired record limitations, consistent cluster passphrase, default applicable CCG component, all-members-running and switch-clusters DR strategies, and source credential reentry when switching. No fixed first-created worker, idle-standby default, universal failover, or exactly-once guarantee. |
| [Managing VAs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/manage_va.html) | Source-to-cluster association, member/component/cluster health views, Connected and Busy meanings, rolling updates and possible long-process interruption, update implications of an unconnected member, scoped operational actions, and documented replacement rather than moving/migrating existing VAs. Deleting/restarting/rebuilding is not prescribed as a first diagnostic action. |
| [System and network requirements](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/requirements_va.html) | Current standard ISC production/sandbox cluster requirement with at least two VAs per cluster; resource/hosting requirements; DNS, time synchronization, HTTPS/cloud destinations, target-specific ports, regional endpoints, and trust requirements. Resource sizes, endpoint/IP lists and port matrices are not copied as a timeless reference. The fictional AssetDesk HTTPS/443 target is not generalized to all sources. |
| [Configuring VAs](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/config_va.html) | Actual deployment mode affects the path; Standard versus HTTP proxy configuration, and source HTTP/HTTPS traffic through the documented proxy mode. Proxy and target trust are separate dependencies; no new inbound exposure, trust bypass, unrestricted firewall opening, or proxy-authentication recipe is recommended. |
| [Customer-provided OS option](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/va/cpos_va.html) | Additional customer OS patching, maintenance and reboot responsibilities while allowing applicable SailPoint software updates. This qualifies standard-image assumptions; the lesson does not reproduce current distribution/version lists, install scripts, commercial options, or legal terms. |
| [Managing sources](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/sources/index.html) | Supporting source/connector and VA-based versus SaaS-connector meanings. A cloud-hosted business application is not sufficient evidence of SaaS execution mode. Connection tests, reads and supported writes have different scopes. |
| [Monitoring provisioning](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/provisioning/tracking.html) | Operation/source/account evidence and reported result versus actual target confirmation. Core supporting behavior retains prior course verification; no actual VA log, task execution or connector transaction was performed. |
| [Access profiles](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/access/access-profiles.html), [request configuration](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/requests/config_ap_roles.html), and [correlation](https://documentation.sailpoint.com/saas/help/accounts/correlation.html) | Supporting one-source entitlement bundle, specified recipient/request review, and account/person association. Read assets is a fictional single-entitlement profile for one existing correctly associated account; no account-creation or multi-account algorithm is generalized. |

### Conditions and limits

- One production cluster C-Asset-Prod contains va-asset-01 and va-asset-02. AssetDesk and Operations Directory are separately configured sources associated with that cluster. Names are teaching labels, not live identifiers, host credentials, or commands.
- Source association, cluster work queue, member execution, cloud communication and target interaction are separate relationships. Polling is documented, but packet order, scheduling fairness, acknowledgement, queue retention, retries and deduplication are not invented.
- The main case supplies host failure of member 01, Connected/cloud polling on member 02, evidence that member 02 handled the operation, target DNS resolution failure from that member, and absent target permission. Without those supplied facts, the failure boundary cannot be presumed.
- DNS failure occurs before target authentication in this case. It is not the credential/privilege-denial incident from ARC-001. Reapproving a request or resetting the recipient password is not inferred as a correction.
- The integration actor svc-asset-isc, recipient asset-32002, and appliance configuration secrets are distinct. Target entitlement asset.read permits reading only under the fictional application model.
- Pairing associates a machine with its tenant/cluster; a saved unpaired record does not establish an operational machine. Cluster passphrase consistency does not make OS logins, recipient passwords and external source credentials interchangeable.
- CCG is the applicable core connector component, not the whole hosted service. Other licensed standalone components and their sizing must be checked separately; none is assumed in the example.
- Outbound cloud connectivity is not a target-network test. Every relevant member must have the needed route, DNS, trust, target connectivity and operation privileges. A laptop test is not a VA-position test.
- Standard ISC core VA requirements are distinguished from IdentityIQ-specific connectivity and feature-specific component sizing. The lesson does not imply that every tenant or source uses VAs.
- HA is scoped to available capacity/member redundancy. Shared hosts, routes, proxies, target systems or other dependencies can defeat continuity. Two VAs are not a speed multiplier or proof of uninterrupted individual work.
- Same-cluster running members and a separately associated DR cluster have different failover behavior. The switch-clusters case requires source reassociation and the documented credential preparation; it is not an automatic tenant-wide worker pool.
- Standard-image vendor software updates coexist with customer hosting/network/monitoring duties. CPOS adds customer OS responsibilities. Update interruption and blocked-update conditions remain explicit; no unsupported manual replacement of managed software is proposed.
- Replacement, restart and source reassociation affect different scopes and require approved procedures. The text is not authorization to mutate real customer infrastructure.
- The lost-response case supplies a possible target-applied change separately from the main DNS failure. Recorded timeout, target state, audit evidence, and supported recovery need to be reconciled; neither universal rollback nor harmless retry is promised.
- Previously collected data can remain visible during a connectivity issue; it is not current target proof, and failed reads alone do not establish employee departure or account deletion.
- Current official documentation uses Identity Services / Human Fabric naming. ISC remains the requested course title; package and component visibility are not license guarantees.

### Diagrams and review checks

- [Source/cluster/member map](../assets/ARC-002_sources_cluster_members.txt): two sources associated with one cluster containing two appliance members; not packet routes or duplicated execution.
- [Outbound communication and work exchange](../assets/ARC-002_outbound_and_work_exchange.txt): VA-initiated cloud/target communication and conceptual request/response work roles.
- Both assets match the inline blocks. Maximum line widths are 45 and 51 characters. Captions/prose explain arrow meaning, different endpoints, response traffic, and omitted private processing details.
- Local and live checks separately verify full text, headings, tables, diagrams, placement, and navigation. These are not observed beginner comprehension or tenant-execution tests.

### Assessment intent and accepted reasoning

| Exercise | Understanding tested | Expected reasoning / acceptable alternatives | Misconception exposed | Source anchor |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| First-created appliance | Cluster association versus fixed worker | Running members poll the cluster; either can handle work in the documented arrangement; inspect actual operation/member evidence | A source is permanently bound to the first appliance | VA deployment; source association |
| Inbound firewall proposal | Work direction versus connection initiation | VA initiates required outbound cloud communication and receives responses; follow supported network design | Cloud-originated work requires unsolicited inbound sessions per action | VA overview; requirements/configuration |
| Connected but unresolved target | Health scope and failure stage | Verify target hostname/DNS/network from the actual member before credential changes; recover and verify operation separately | Connected validates every source dependency or an upstream DNS failure is a target-password failure | VA health; network requirements |
| Healthy separate DR cluster | Deployment-strategy-specific failover | Switch-clusters strategy requires source reassociation and credential preparation plus target-path validation | Every healthy cluster is automatically available to every source | Deployment DR strategies |
| Rolling update claim | Availability versus individual process outcome | Long work can be interrupted; inspect result and supported recovery instead of assuming uninterrupted completion | Two members guarantee every running aggregation survives updates | VA management |
| Target changed, response lost | Actual outcome versus recorded completion | Compare target/audit evidence and reconcile supported recovery before retry; no timeout-equals-no-effect assumption | Every timeout proves no change or universal rollback | Monitoring; supplied fictional target/response facts |
| Payroll shared-route transfer | Shared dependencies and stale observations | Both members can reach cloud while shared target route fails; inspect both member paths and affected work; recover network and verify relevant read/write outcomes | Appliance count removes common dependencies or old collected data is a live target test | Requirements; deployment HA; monitoring |

Questions and worked reasoning remain in the learner file. Reference evidence, assessment intent, and review limitations stay outside it.
